## 🚨 Critical Vulnerabilities

 * **Unauthenticated identifier-only account takeover via `POST /api/users/resolve-id`.** No `JwtAuthGuard`. Supplying a known `email`, `google_id`, or `firebase_uid` looks up `user_profiles`, optionally **links** a caller-supplied `firebase_uid`/`google_id` onto that row, then **mints a full JWT pair** (`accessToken` + `refreshToken`) for that user. If Admin SDK is initialized and no local row matches, `tryAutoCreateUserFromFirebase` calls `admin.auth().getUser(firebase_uid)` **without an ID token** and provisions a KC user + tokens from that UID. Concrete trigger: `POST /api/users/resolve-id` with `{ "email": "<victim>" }` returns tokens with the victim’s `roles`. SRS §2.1.6 currently documents this minting behavior — the spec itself should be tightened to require proof of possession (verified Firebase/Google ID token or existing session), not identifier matching. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (183–188) → `apps/api/src/services/user-auth.service.ts` (`resolveUserId` 270–342, `tryAutoCreateUserFromFirebase` 450–575, `linkResolveIdentifiers` 580–627, `buildResolveSuccessResponse` 645–672) -> Action: Remove token minting from resolve-id; require a verified ID token; never link identifiers from an unauthenticated body; bind any remaining resolve helper to `JwtAuthGuard`.

 * **Primary mobile login accepts email-only (password verification skipped, including empty string).** `POST /api/users/login` (`UsersController` / `UserAuthService.loginUser`) verifies argon2 **only when both** `loginData.password` and `user.password_hash` are truthy (223–231). Omitting `password`, sending `password: ""` (JS-falsy), or targeting a row with a null hash, falls through to `success: true` and returns email, phone, roles, `parent_manager_id`, and other profile fields. `/auth/login` correctly rejects missing hash and invalid passwords (528–541). Concrete trigger A: `POST /api/users/login` with `{ "email": "<victim>" }` (no password). Concrete trigger B: `{ "email": "<victim>", "password": "" }`. Combined with resolve-id, the documented mobile sequence (`apiService.loginUser` then `resolveUserId`) is an email-only takeover. Contrasts SRS §2.1.2 (`argon2.verify`). - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (116–118); `apps/api/src/services/user-auth.service.ts` (190–263, especially 223–231); contrast `apps/api/src/auth/controllers/auth.controller.ts` (501–541); mobile `apps/mobile/utils/apiService.ts` (463–467) / `dbConfig.ts` (`USER_LOGIN`) -> Action: Always require a non-empty password; fail closed if hash is missing; never return `success: true` without `argon2.verify`; stop treating resolve-id as a post-login token mint.

 * **Unauthenticated root-admin bootstrap via committed fallback email (plus identity-source split).** `KC_ROOT_ADMIN_EMAIL_FALLBACK` is committed in `kc-root-admin.defaults.ts`. Unauthenticated `POST /api/users/register` with that email sets `initialRoles = ["user", "admin"]` (101–102) when the row does not yet exist. Unauthenticated `POST /api/users/login` with the same email runs `array_append(roles, 'admin')` **before** the optional password check (212–220), then succeeds without a password (223–231). **Identity split:** `UserProfileService.getRootAdminEmail()` (52–56) reads only `ROOT_ADMIN_EMAIL` (empty string if unset) and is used solely to skip `roles` writes (349–361). The PUT role/hierarchy **block** (281–297) and unauthenticated `GET` listings compare a **hardcoded Gmail literal**, not the env var and not the shared constant. Concrete trigger A (empty DB / missing row): `POST /api/users/register` with the fallback email (password optional) creates an admin. Concrete trigger B (row exists): `POST /api/users/login` with `{ "email": "<fallback>" }` (no password) writes `admin` if missing, returns PII, then `POST /api/users/resolve-id` mints an admin JWT. Concrete trigger C (misconfiguration): setting `ROOT_ADMIN_EMAIL` to a different mailbox does **not** stop bootstrap of the fallback address and does **not** auto-promote the env mailbox. SRS §2.1.1 allows first-login promotion only as a bootstrap — not as an unauthenticated, passwordless grant. - File: `apps/api/src/config/kc-root-admin.defaults.ts`; `apps/api/src/services/user-auth.service.ts` (101–102, 212–231); `apps/api/src/services/user-profile.service.ts` (52–56, 281–297, 349–361); `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (111–118, 183–188) -> Action: Single source of truth for root-admin identity; require a verified secret/env match plus password (or disable public register for that email); never mutate roles before `argon2.verify`; bind bootstrap to a one-shot setup command, not a public API.

 * **Unauthenticated register binds attacker-chosen Firebase UID.** `POST /api/users/register` has no guard. `RegisterUserBody` accepts `firebase_uid` and `id`; the INSERT writes `userData.firebase_uid || userData.id || null` into `user_profiles.firebase_uid` (139). Password is optional (97–99). Concrete trigger: `POST /api/users/register` with `{ "email": "attacker@example.com", "firebase_uid": "<victim Firebase UID>" }` (or `{ "id": "<victim Firebase UID>" }`). The victim’s later Firebase ID-token path (`JwtAuthGuard` lookup by `firebase_uid`) attaches the attacker’s KC row. Complements resolve-id linking and profile `firebase_uid` mass-assignment as a **fourth** unauthenticated identity-binding path. - File: `apps/api/src/services/user-auth.service.ts` (`RegisterUserBody` 20–33, hash optional 97–99, bind at 139); `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (111–113) -> Action: Ignore client `firebase_uid`/`id` on register; require password; bind Firebase UID only after verified ID-token proof.

 * **Firebase JWKS fallback accepts any Firebase project.** When the Admin SDK is not initialized, `verifyIdTokenWithPublicJwks` takes `aud`/`iss` from the token and only compares them to `FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID` **if that env var is set**. File header states this is intentional. Concrete trigger: attacker creates their own Firebase project, obtains a valid Google-signed ID token, then (via unauthenticated `resolve-id` linking, register `firebase_uid` bind, or authenticated `PUT /api/users/:id` `firebase_uid` mass-assignment) binds that UID to a victim and presents the ID token to any `JwtAuthGuard` route. Complements resolve-id/mass-assignment with a **second login mechanism** that never mints a server JWT. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/services/firebase-admin.service.ts` (11–14, 150–185, 191–264); `apps/api/src/auth/guards/jwt-auth.guard.ts` (117–134, 170–213) -> Action: Require `FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID` (or service-account `project_id`) and reject tokens whose `aud` does not match; fail closed if unset in deployed environments.

 * **Google OAuth access-token fallback bypasses ID-token audience and email verification.** `POST /auth/google` verifies an ID token with `googleClient.verifyIdToken` and rejects unverified emails, but if verification fails **or** `idToken` is omitted, it falls back to `GET https://www.googleapis.com/oauth2/v3/userinfo` with a raw Bearer **access token** and hardcodes `emailVerified: true` (687–695). No `aud` binding to `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`. Concrete trigger: any Google OAuth access token (from an unrelated Google Cloud client) that can call userinfo mints a KC JWT. Third unauthenticated login path besides resolve-id and Firebase JWKS. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/controllers/auth.controller.ts` (613–698, especially 660–695) -> Action: Require a verified ID token with explicit `audience`; never accept access-token userinfo as a login proof; do not force `emailVerified: true`.

 * **Profile mass-assignment + password IDOR on `PUT /api/users/:id`.** `UpdateUserBody` accepts `password`, `roles`, `firebase_uid`, `parent_manager_id`, `hierarchy_level`. Controller uses `JwtAuthGuard` only — **no ownership check and no `req.user`**. Lookup is `id::text = $1 OR LOWER(email) = LOWER($1) OR firebase_uid = $1 OR google_id = $1` (267). Root-admin block (281–297) covers **only** `roles` / `parent_manager_id` / `hierarchy_level`; `password` is hashed and written (303–307) and `firebase_uid` is written (345–347) even for the root-admin row. `parent_manager_id`/`hierarchy_level` are on the DTO and blocked for root-admin but **not written** to SQL for other users. Concrete trigger: any JWT (including one minted by resolve-id) `PUT /api/users/<victim-uuid-or-email>` with `{ "password": "...", "roles": ["admin"], "firebase_uid": "<attacker>" }` resets the victim’s password, escalates roles (except the hardcoded root-admin email’s roles), and hijacks Firebase identity. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (126–133); `apps/api/src/services/user-profile.service.ts` (`UpdateUserBody` 18–32, lookup 267, root-admin block 281–297, password 303–307, firebase_uid/roles 345–361) -> Action: Bind updates to `req.user.userId`; reject `roles`/`firebase_uid` except via admin-only endpoints; require current password (or admin) to change password; do not resolve PUT targets by email/firebase_uid/google_id.

 * **JWT-gated donation create spoofs `donor_id` and can inflate another user’s donation totals.** `POST /api/donations` uses `JwtAuthGuard` but never reads `req.user`. INSERT uses `donationData.donor_id` (503). When `type === "money"` and `amount` is set, a second statement does `total_donations_amount = total_donations_amount + $1` for that `donor_id` (525–534). Controller TODOs at 481–484 already note missing authorization. Concrete trigger: any JWT `POST /api/donations` with `{ "donor_id": "<victim-uuid>", "type": "money", "amount": 999999, "title": "...", "category_id": "<id>" }` attributes a donation to the victim and increases their public donation total. Complements unauthenticated `GET /api/stats/analytics/categories` money SUM. Contrasts `ChallengesController` / `CommunityGroupChallengesController`, which bind actor from `CurrentUser`. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/donations.controller.ts` (478–534) -> Action: Set `donor_id` exclusively from `req.user.userId`; ignore client donor/recipient identity fields except where an admin flow is explicitly authorized.

 * **JWT-gated donation PUT/DELETE is an unscoped IDOR.** `PUT /api/donations/:id` (698–744) and `DELETE /api/donations/:id` (746+) use `JwtAuthGuard` only. Update applies `title`, `description`, `amount`, `status`, images, tags, metadata with **no** `donor_id` / `req.user` comparison. Delete loads `id, status` then proceeds without an owner or admin check. Concrete trigger: any JWT `PUT /api/donations/<id>` with `{ "status": "cancelled", "amount": 0 }` or `DELETE /api/donations/<id>` alters or removes another user’s donation. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/donations.controller.ts` (698–746) -> Action: Require `req.user.userId === donor_id` or `AdminAuthGuard`; do not accept client `status`/`amount` on a generic update without an explicit admin/owner policy.

 * **Unauthenticated donation-detail PII.** `GET /api/donations/:id` has no guard. The SELECT joins `user_profiles` and returns `up.phone as donor_phone, up.email as donor_email` (676–685) in addition to donation row fields (`d.*`). List `GET /api/donations` is also unauthenticated but only exposes donor name/city/avatar (596–598). Concrete trigger: `GET /api/donations/<uuid>` returns that donor’s phone and email (cached 15 minutes under `donation_<id>`). Violates SRS §3.5 data minimization. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/donations.controller.ts` (667–696) -> Action: Auth-gate detail; never project `phone`/`email` to non-self, non-admin, non-operator callers; strip those columns from the public cache payload.

 * **JWT-gated task hours-report IDOR (new 2026-08-29).** `GET /api/tasks/hours-report/:managerId` uses `JwtAuthGuard` only. `getHoursReport` never reads `req.user`. It resolves `managerId` (UUID **or** email / `firebase_uid`) to a UUID, walks the recursive `parent_manager_id` subordinate tree, and returns `manager_hours`, `team_total_hours`, `by_task` (task id/title/hours), `by_period`, and `by_user` (user id/name/hours). Concrete trigger: mint any JWT (resolve-id is enough), scrape manager IDs from unauthenticated `GET /api/users/hierarchy/tree` or `GET /api/users`, then `GET /api/tasks/hours-report/<manager-uuid-or-email>` to read another org’s time-tracking. Complements unscoped `GET /api/tasks`. Contrasts `NotificationsController.validateOwnership` and `ChallengesController` owner-scoped queries. - File: `apps/api/src/tasks/tasks.controller.ts` (568–587); `apps/api/src/tasks/tasks-read.service.ts` (151–311) -> Action: Bind the path param to `req.user.userId` (or require `AdminAuthGuard` / manager-of-self check); do not accept email/`firebase_uid` as a public manager key.

 * **Unauthenticated user directory and PII.** `GET /api/users` and `GET /api/users/:id` have no guards. `getUserById` SELECTs `email` and `phone` and matches UUID **or** email / `firebase_uid` / `google_id` (126–157). `getUsers` SELECTs `u.email` (515) plus roles, manager email, and hierarchy. Also unauthenticated: `GET search` (returns `id, name, email, avatar_url, roles` for `is_active` users), `GET :id/activities`, `GET :id/stats`, `GET stats/summary`. Concrete trigger: `GET /api/users` dumps the directory; `GET /api/users/<email>` returns that user’s phone; `GET /api/users/search?q=ab` enumerates emails. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (40–43, 101–163, 121–123, 135–149); `apps/api/src/services/user-profile.service.ts` (search 68–77, getUserById ~126–157, getUsers ~515) -> Action: Require auth; redact phone/email/roles for non-self, non-admin callers; stop identifier-OR lookup on a public GET.

 * **Client-supplied hierarchy actors skip authorization when omitted or not found.** `setManagerEnsureRequesterAdminIfProvided` returns `null` (allow) when `requestingUserId` is omitted **or** the user row is not found (492–517). Promote and `manageHierarchy` trust body admin/manager IDs with no JWT binding. `GET eligible-for-promotion/:adminId`, `GET hierarchy/tree`, and `GET :id/hierarchy` have **no** `UseGuards`. Concrete trigger: omit `requestingAdminId` / `requestingUserId` and mutate hierarchy; or scrape the full tree unauthenticated. SRS §2.2.3 documents these as `JwtAuthGuard`. - File: `apps/api/src/services/user-hierarchy.service.ts` (492–517, 871–914, 945–980); `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (91–108) -> Action: Require `JwtAuthGuard` + `AdminAuthGuard`; bind actor exclusively from `req.user.userId`; fail closed if the actor is missing.

 * **Spoofable follow graph.** `POST/DELETE /api/users/:id/follow` are JWT-gated but `UserFollowService` inserts/deletes using body `follower_id`, not `req.user.userId`. Concrete trigger: authenticated user A follows/unfollows as user B. - File: `apps/api/src/services/user-follow.service.ts` (body `follower_id`); `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (165–180) -> Action: Bind `follower_id` exclusively from the JWT.

 * **Unauthenticated chat writes impersonate `sender_id`.** Class-level `OptionalAuthGuard` always returns `true` (338). Unauthenticated `POST /api/chat/messages` uses body `sender_id` (456–464). Authenticated callers that send a mismatched `sender_id` are rejected (450–455); guests are not. Mark-read falls back to `body.user_id`. Concrete trigger: `POST /api/chat/messages` with `{ "sender_id": "<victim>", "content": "..." }` (and a conversation id / participants) inserts a message attributed to the victim. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/guards/jwt-auth.guard.ts` (308–339); `apps/api/src/controllers/chat.controller.ts` (46, 441–465, 679, 759) -> Action: Require `JwtAuthGuard` on write (and preferably read) paths; never accept client `sender_id`.

 * **Unguarded mutating APIs (rides, items, CRM, community-members, collections, dedicated-items, rate-limit).** No `UseGuards` on `RidesController` (book spoofs `passenger_id` 374–468), `ItemsDeliveryController` (create with caller `owner_id`), `DedicatedItemsController`, `CrmController`, `CommunityMembersController`, `ItemsController` collections CRUD (`POST/PUT/DELETE` 80–108; allowlist includes `users`, `chats`, `messages`, `tasks`, `notifications`, `rides`, `organizations`), and `RateLimitController`. Redis-test (get/set/delete/keys) is registered **only when `NODE_ENV !== production`** (`app.module.ts` 122) — Railway `railway.toml.dev` uses `NODE_ENV=development`, so it is live on that service. Concrete trigger: unauthenticated `POST /api/rides/:id/book` with another user’s `passenger_id`. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/rides.controller.ts`; `items-delivery.controller.ts`; `apps/api/src/items/dedicated-items.controller.ts`; `crm.controller.ts`; `community-members.controller.ts`; `apps/api/src/items/items.controller.ts`; `rate-limit.controller.ts`; `redis-test.controller.ts`; `app.module.ts` (122) -> Action: `JwtAuthGuard` (admin where appropriate) on all mutating routes; bind actor IDs from JWT; remove redis-test and rate-limit demo routes from deployed environments.

 * **JwtAuthGuard rate-limit key is the shared HS256 header prefix — and unauthenticated `/rate-limit/custom` can plant a platform-wide block.** `checkRateLimit(token.substring(0, 16), "api_access")` (96–114). Issued header is always `{alg:HS256,typ:JWT}`, so the first 16 chars are always `eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1`. 100 req/60s then 5-minute block is **global for all JWT users**. Thrown as `UnauthorizedException` (401). Mobile `authFetchInterceptor` treats 401 as logout (75–89). `DELETE /rate-limit/clear` already deletes both window and block keys for an attacker-chosen identifier. `POST /rate-limit/custom` has no guard and passes client `rule` + `customKey` into `applyCustomRateLimit` (`rate-limit.controller.ts` 233–264; `rate-limit.service.ts` 269–276). Redis keys are `blocked:{ruleType}:{identifier}`. Concrete trigger: two unauthenticated `POST /rate-limit/custom` bodies `{ "identifier": "eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1", "customKey": "api_access", "rule": { "requests": 1, "windowMs": 1000, "blockDurationMs": 86400000 } }` write `blocked:api_access:eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1` for 24 hours; every subsequent `JwtAuthGuard` request 401s and mobile clients clear session. Complementary clear: `DELETE /rate-limit/clear` with `{ "identifier": "eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1", "ruleType": "api_access" }` lifts the same bucket. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/guards/jwt-auth.guard.ts` (96–114); `apps/api/src/auth/services/jwt.service.ts` (276–281); `apps/api/src/auth/services/rate-limit.service.ts` (82–141, 181–192, 269–276); `apps/api/src/controllers/rate-limit.controller.ts` (155–167, 233–264); `apps/mobile/auth/interceptors/authFetchInterceptor.ts` (46–89) -> Action: Rate-limit by `userId` (after verify) or a hash of the full token; return 429 not 401; remove `/rate-limit/*` demo routes from deployed environments (or `AdminAuthGuard` + no client-supplied `customKey`/`rule`).

 * **Admin-files list ACL is JWT-only.** `GET /api/admin-files` and `GET folders` use `JwtAuthGuard`; POST/DELETE correctly use `AdminAuthGuard`. Concrete trigger: any authenticated user lists Finance/HR file URLs. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/admin-files.controller.ts` (80–140 vs 142–203) -> Action: Use `AdminAuthGuard` on GET/folders as well.

 * **Unauthenticated donation analytics and real-time presence.** `GET /api/stats/analytics/categories` returns donation money SUM with no guard (439–496). `GET /api/stats/real-time` returns `users_online` and last-hour `user_activities` (782–831). `analytics/users` correctly uses `AdminAuthGuard`. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/stats.controller.ts` (439–496, 782–831) -> Action: Auth-gate (admin for money totals); do not expose presence without auth.

 * **Demo session controller: unauthenticated session enumeration and forced logout.** Demo `SessionController` (`/session`) has no guards. `POST /session/login` verifies real `password_hash` via argon2. `GET /session/user/:userId` lists sessions; `DELETE /session/logout/:sessionId` and `DELETE /session/logout-all/:userId` destroy sessions without proving possession. Concrete trigger: `DELETE /session/logout-all/<victim-uuid>` signs the victim out of all devices. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/controllers/session.controller.ts` (27–229) -> Action: Remove from production/dev deployments; if kept for tests, bind to `JwtAuthGuard` + ownership and disable login.

 * **Unauthenticated Google Places proxy and unencoded `place_id`.** `GET /autocomplete`, `/place-details`, `/search-stats` have no guards and spend `GOOGLE_API_KEY`. `/place-details` interpolates `place_id` without `encodeURIComponent` (line 75), so `&`-bearing values append extra query parameters to Google’s API. `/search-stats` enumerates Redis `search_activity:*` / `places:autocomplete:*` keys. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/places.controller.ts` (9–103) -> Action: Auth + throttle; encode `place_id`; hide search-stats behind admin.

 * **Request-path and startup `DROP TABLE ... CASCADE`.** `PostsController.migrateExistingPostsTableColumns` / `ensurePostsTable` can `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS posts CASCADE` on a live request if `id`/`author_id` are missing (line 78). Related drops: `post_likes` (309), `comment_likes` + `post_comments` (373–374). `tasks-schema.service.ts:41` drops `posts` via task side-effects. `DatabaseInit` drops legacy JSONB `donations` (568) and `rides` (670) on `onModuleInit` when a `data` column is detected. Concrete trigger: a schema-compat mismatch (or a poisoned `information_schema` view in a compromised DB role) destroys posts/likes/comments and their FKs during ordinary traffic or process boot. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/posts.controller.ts` (78, 309, 373–374); `apps/api/src/tasks/tasks-schema.service.ts` (41); `apps/api/src/database/database.init.ts` (568, 670) -> Action: Never DROP on the request path; replace with expand-only migrations; gate startup drops behind an explicit one-shot env flag.

 * **Committed credentials and PII backups in git.** `apps/api/railway.toml.dev` hardcodes a development `JWT_SECRET` (line 34). `docs/SSOT/runbooks/api/environment-separation.md` embeds a production `JWT_SECRET` (line 126; line 102 is a placeholder). Values are not repeated here. `apps/api/src/main.ts` (149–218) fingerprints environments by matching **database password and Redis password/host substrings** committed in source — rotate those credentials and replace the check with an explicit `ENVIRONMENT` flag. 49 tracked JSON files under `apps/api/data-backups/20251224-162421/` include `user_profiles.json` whose keys include `email`, `phone`, and `password_hash`. The env cross-check does not prevent these files from shipping. - File: `apps/api/railway.toml.dev:34`; `docs/SSOT/runbooks/api/environment-separation.md:126`; `apps/api/src/main.ts` (149–218); `apps/api/data-backups/20251224-162421/*` -> Action: Rotate all exposed JWT secrets and DB/Redis credentials whose fragments appear in git; purge secrets from git history; gitignore backups; use secret managers only.

 * **JWT-gated but unscoped task list.** `GET /api/tasks` uses `JwtAuthGuard` then lists by query filters (`assignee`, `status`, …) with **no** restriction to the caller’s tasks. Any authenticated user can enumerate all tasks (ClosedRoute even queries another profile’s `assignee`). - File: `apps/api/src/tasks/tasks.controller.ts` (74–146) -> Action: Default-scope to `req.user`; require admin for global lists.

 * **Unthrottled primary mobile login/register.** SRS §2.1.2 and §3.1 require `ThrottlerGuard` on auth. `AuthController` (`/auth/*`) has class-level `ThrottlerGuard`, but the **primary mobile path** (`apiService.ts` / `dbConfig.USER_LOGIN`) calls `POST /api/users/login` and `POST /api/users/register` on `UsersController`, which have **no** throttle and no global `APP_GUARD`. Concrete trigger: unbounded credential stuffing against `/api/users/login` (and account-creation spam on `/api/users/register`) bypasses the documented 60 req/60s auth limit. Magnified by the password-skip finding: stuffing is unnecessary if email alone (or `password: ""`) succeeds. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (111–118); `apps/mobile/utils/apiService.ts` (463–467); `apps/mobile/utils/dbConfig.ts`; `apps/api/src/app.module.ts` (72–77) -> Action: Apply `ThrottlerGuard` (stricter login tier) to `/api/users/login` and `/register`; register global `APP_GUARD`; keep `/auth/*` and `/api/users/*` auth surfaces consistent.

 * **JWT refresh persists stale roles.** SRS §2.1.4 requires refresh to **fetch latest roles from the database** so demotions propagate. `JwtService.refreshAccessToken` copies `payload.roles` from the presented refresh token and issues a new access token with those roles; there is no `user_profiles` lookup. Redis existence of the refresh token **is** checked inside `verifyToken` (123–129). HMAC compare uses `!==` rather than `timingSafeEqual` (308). Concrete trigger: an admin is demoted to `user` while still holding a valid 30-day refresh token; `POST /auth/refresh` continues to mint access tokens with `roles: ["admin"]` until that refresh token expires or is revoked in Redis. Complements profile mass-assignment (attacker can also *gain* roles) by letting a formerly privileged user *keep* them after a legitimate demotion. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/services/jwt.service.ts` (146–177, especially 157–165, compare at 308); `apps/api/src/auth/controllers/auth.controller.ts` (981–998) -> Action: On refresh, load `roles` from `user_profiles` by `payload.userId`; reject refresh if the user is inactive; use `crypto.timingSafeEqual`; consider rotating the refresh token.

---

## 🐞 Bugs & Logic Issues

 * **JWT-gated task hour log spoofs body `user_id`.** `POST /api/tasks/:id/log-hours` uses `JwtAuthGuard` but never reads `req.user`. It resolves body `user_id` to a UUID and UPSERTs `task_time_logs.actual_hours` for that user (`ON CONFLICT (task_id, user_id) DO UPDATE`). Concrete trigger: any JWT `POST /api/tasks/<id>/log-hours` with `{ "hours": 8, "user_id": "<victim-uuid>" }` overwrites the victim’s logged hours (or inserts a row attributed to them). Combined with the new hours-report IDOR, an attacker can both write and read another user’s time-tracking. - Recommendation: Bind `user_id` exclusively from the JWT; ignore body actor fields. File: `apps/api/src/tasks/tasks.controller.ts` (384–445).

 * **Hours-report errors are swallowed as empty success (new 2026-08-29).** `TasksReadService.getHoursReport` catch (312–323) and `TasksController.getHoursReport` catch (574–586) both return `{ success: true, data: { manager_hours: 0, … empty arrays } }` instead of `success: false`. A DB/Redis failure or unexpected exception is indistinguishable from “this manager has no hours.” - Recommendation: Return `success: false` with a generic error; log the real exception server-side. File: `apps/api/src/tasks/tasks-read.service.ts` (312–323); `apps/api/src/tasks/tasks.controller.ts` (574–586).

 * **Items-delivery `GET /requests` is shadowed (new 2026-08-29).** `ItemsDeliveryController` registers parameterized `GET :id` (180) **before** static `GET requests` (216). Nest matches the first compatible 1-segment GET, so `GET /api/items-delivery/requests` calls `getItemById("requests")` rather than `getItemRequests`. `POST requests` is 1-segment vs `POST :id/reserve` (2-segment) and does not collide. - Recommendation: Declare `GET requests` before `GET :id`. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/items-delivery.controller.ts` (180–223).

 * **Dedicated-items `GET /search` is shadowed (new 2026-08-29).** `DedicatedItemsController` registers `GET :id` (69) **before** static `GET search` (166). `GET /api/dedicated-items/search` therefore looks up item id `"search"` and typically returns “Item not found.” `GET owner/:ownerId` and `GET category/:category` are 2-segment and still reachable. - Recommendation: Declare `GET search` before `GET :id`. File: `apps/api/src/items/dedicated-items.controller.ts` (69–186).

 * **Spoofable post comment likes.** `POST /api/posts/:postId/comments/:commentId/like` is JWT-gated but reads `body.user_id` exclusively (2163–2209); `req.user.userId` is never consulted. Concrete trigger: any JWT with `{ "user_id": "<victim-uuid>" }` toggles the victim’s comment like and can notify the comment author as that user. - Recommendation: Bind `user_id` exclusively from the JWT. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/posts.controller.ts` (2152–2217).

 * **Spoofable post comments.** `POST /api/posts/:postId/comments` is JWT-gated but requires body `user_id` (1573–1584) and never uses `req.user`. Same family as likes. - Recommendation: Bind comment author from the JWT. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/posts.controller.ts` (1573–1584).

 * **Unauthenticated rate-limit stress-test holds connections for attacker-chosen `delayMs`.** `POST /rate-limit/stress-test` has no guard. `requests` is capped at 50, but `delayMs` is used as `body.delayMs || 100` with **no upper bound** (`rate-limit.controller.ts` 61–97). The loop `await`s `setTimeout(delayMs)` between iterations, so one request can pin an HTTP connection until `httpServer.setTimeout(30000)` (`main.ts` 441–443) kills it. Concrete trigger: parallel `POST /rate-limit/stress-test` with `{ "requests": 50, "delayMs": 60000 }` (effective hold ~30s each) plus Redis writes via `checkRateLimit`. Combined with `/rate-limit/custom` this is a cheap unauthenticated DoS / Redis-amplification surface. - Recommendation: Remove the controller from deployed environments; if kept, `AdminAuthGuard`, cap `delayMs` (e.g. ≤ 20), and do not accept client `identifier`/`ruleType`/`customKey`.

 * **Spoofable post likes.** `POST /api/posts/:postId/like` is JWT-gated but reads `body.user_id` exclusively (1345–1406); `req.user.userId` is never consulted. Like/unlike and `notifyAuthorOfNewPostLike` therefore run as any UUID the caller supplies. Same actor-binding gap as comments. Concrete trigger: any JWT `POST /api/posts/<id>/like` with `{ "user_id": "<victim-uuid>" }` toggles the victim’s like and can notify the author as that user. - Recommendation: Bind `user_id` exclusively from the JWT; ignore body actor fields. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/posts.controller.ts` (1343–1406).

 * **Ride booking approval can oversell seats.** `updateBookingStatus` sets status to `approved` then only marks the ride `full` when remaining ≤ 0; no capacity reject and no `FOR UPDATE` row lock before approve. Concurrent approvals can exceed `available_seats`. Booking create does check remaining seats (407–410) but that check is not applied on approve. - Recommendation: In one transaction, lock the ride + sum approved seats; reject approve when `seats_requested` would exceed remaining capacity. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/rides.controller.ts` (505–555).

 * **Unauthenticated real-time stats cartesian join.** `GET /api/stats/real-time` has no guard (already critical-adjacent for `users_online` + last-hour activity). The donations/rides subquery is `FROM donations d FULL OUTER JOIN rides r ON 1=1`, a full cartesian product. Concrete trigger: with D donation rows and R ride rows, Postgres materializes D×R rows before `COUNT(CASE WHEN d.status = 'active')`, so `active_donations` is inflated by ~R and `active_rides` by ~D. As tables grow this is also an unauthenticated CPU/memory DoS. - Recommendation: Replace with two independent `COUNT(*)` queries (or `UNION ALL` of aggregates); auth-gate the endpoint. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/stats.controller.ts` (782–831, join at 803–809).

 * **Collections static GET routes are shadowed.** `ItemsController` registers parameterized `GET :collection` (line 43) **before** static `GET popular-collections` (136) and `GET cache-stats` (153). Nest matches the first compatible 1-segment GET, so those helpers run `validateCollection` against the path name and return `400 Invalid collection` (`popular-collections` / `cache-stats` are not in `ALLOWED_COLLECTIONS`). `GET user-activity/:userId` is 2-segment and still reachable. Same family as the new items-delivery / dedicated-items shadows. - Recommendation: Declare static paths before `:collection`; or nest helpers under a non-colliding prefix. File: `apps/api/src/items/items.controller.ts` (43–77 vs 136–167).

 * **ClosedRoute task-type mismatch hides the duplicate-skip.** Profile closed tab loads done/archived tasks and skips rows whose `c.type === 'task'`, but newly pushed items use `type: 'task_post'`. Assignment posts already in `allContent` with a different type therefore appear twice. - Recommendation: Compare `taskData.id` regardless of `type`, or use one canonical type. File: `apps/mobile/bottomBarScreens/profile/ClosedRoute.tsx` (322–351).

 * **Auth refresh waiters hang after a failed refresh.** `fetchWithAuth` queues concurrent 401s on `refreshSubscribers`. On refresh failure the first caller sets `isRefreshing = false` and **clears** `refreshSubscribers` without invoking callbacks (75–78). Waiters remain pending forever. Rate-limit 401s (shared HS256 header) amplify this into a mobile hang + forced logout. - Recommendation: Reject queued waiters on failure (`onRefreshed` with null / reject the Promise); never treat 429-class limits as 401. File: `apps/mobile/auth/interceptors/authFetchInterceptor.ts` (9–90).

 * **Task completion can insert a second post.** `finalizePerformerCompletionPost` UPDATEs an assignment post when title matches `משימה חדשה:` / `יצרת משימה חדשה:`; otherwise INSERTs a new `task_completion` row (441–477). Repeat PATCH-to-done (or title mismatch) creates duplicate feed posts. - Recommendation: Unique `(task_id, author_id, post_type)` or always UPDATE-or-skip. File: `apps/api/src/tasks/tasks-side-effects.service.ts` (441–477).

 * **Client-facing `error.message` leaks.** Posts, tasks, dedicated-items, items, and `JwtAuthGuard.verifyFirebaseToken` return raw `error.message` to clients. Contrasts `constraints.md` / NFR error bubbling. - Recommendation: Map to stable public codes; log details server-side only.

 * **Public knowledge-link POST remains unauthenticated.** `POST /api/donations/knowledge/links` has no guard (SRS §2.3.3 allows public add). DELETE correctly checks admin roles in-handler. Combined with no throttle this is spam / phishing-link injection. - Recommendation: Auth-gate or throttle + abuse reporting; keep list public if product requires it. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/donations.controller.ts` (226–302 vs 305–318).

---

## 🏗️ Architecture & Clean Code

 * **Clean Architecture target is unmet.** `docs/SSOT/CODE_QUALITY/layers-and-boundaries.md` requires Infrastructure → Application → Domain. There are **zero** `/domain`, `/application`, or `/infrastructure` folders under `apps/` or `packages/`. Nest feature folders own SQL, Redis, and HTTP in the same files. Closest pure islands: `apps/api/src/tasks/task-completion-participants.ts`, `tasks-validation.ts`, `tasks-query-params.ts`, `tasks.types.ts` (no Nest/`pg` imports). `packages/` only contains `config-eslint`.

 * **Fat controllers still own policy + SQL.** Confirmed LOC this run: `posts.controller.ts` 2741 (Pool + Redis + DDL), `stats.controller.ts` 1709, `user-hierarchy.service.ts` 1675, `database.init.ts` 1374, `community-group-challenges.controller.ts` 1097, `donations.controller.ts` 892, `chat.controller.ts` 813, `rides.controller.ts` 779. `items-delivery.service.ts` is misplaced under `controllers/`. Positive in-folder contrasts: `ChallengesController` / `CommunityGroupChallengesController` bind actor from `CurrentUser`; `NotificationsController.validateOwnership`; `UsersController` is thin but services still inject `pg` Pool.

 * **SRS / code mismatches (prefer SRS; log as gaps).** §2.1.2 and §3.1 require throttle + `argon2.verify` on login — `/api/users/login` has neither. §2.1.4 requires refresh to reload DB roles — `refreshAccessToken` copies token roles. §2.1.1 bootstrap is specified as `ROOT_ADMIN_EMAIL` / `super_admin` on first login — implementation uses a committed fallback email, `admin` (not `super_admin`), and passwordless grant. §2.2.1 says profile updates are restricted to authenticated users **or admin** — PUT has JWT but no ownership. §2.2.3 documents hierarchy endpoints as `JwtAuthGuard` — tree / eligible-for-promotion / `:id/hierarchy` are unguarded. §2.1.6 documents resolve-id token minting (spec should be tightened). §3.2 says admin access is role-based only, no hardcoded email checks — PUT block uses a Gmail literal. §3.1 global ThrottlerGuard is not registered as `APP_GUARD`.

 * **ValidationPipe is global but bypassed.** `main.ts` 427–436 sets `whitelist` + `forbidNonWhitelisted`, but most mutating controllers type `@Body()` as `Record<string, unknown>` or inline interfaces without class-validator metadata, so mass-assignment is unconstrained. `ChallengesController` is a better contrast (class DTOs + `validate()`).

 * **Operator matching module is specified, not implemented.** SRS §3.2 / §10 describe `OperatorAuthGuard` and `/api/operator/*`. No such controller exists. Do not treat absence as a vulnerability in this pass; it is an SRS gap.

Same non-determinism family as 2026-08-14 through 2026-08-28 (those days observed 45→95→96 then 44→94→95). Largest tracked source: `apps/mobile/utils/bubbles_Data.tsx` (4852). Mobile hubs: `databaseService.ts` 1225, `SecureApiService.ts` 1165, `apiService.ts` 1055, `enhancedDatabaseService.ts` 897. Duplicate API clients (`apiService` vs `SecureApiService`) and duplicate feed mappers (`mapPostToFeedItemForItemsScreen` / `ForTrumpScreen` vs `useFeedData`) remain.

 * **tech-debt-log.md overlap.** Explicitly logged: `databaseService.ts` large legacy surface (2026-05-03); orphan donation wrappers; admin tables API without mobile UI; Maestro E2E; production APM. **Not** logged as named bugs: hours-report IDOR, items-delivery/dedicated-items route shadows, auth refresh waiters, ClosedRoute task type, task completion post duplicates, ride approval oversell, SecureApiService duplication, fat controllers / missing CA folders, Places unauth proxy, session logout-all, unthrottled `/api/users/login`, password-optional login (including empty string), register firebase_uid/id bind, unauthenticated root-admin bootstrap (identity trifurcation), unauthenticated stats analytics/real-time, JWT refresh stale roles, unauthenticated chat POST /messages sender impersonation, Firebase JWKS any-project, JwtAuthGuard shared HS256-header rate-limit bucket plus `/rate-limit/custom` plant-block, Google `/auth/google` access-token fallback, stats cartesian `ON 1=1` join, profile password IDOR, committed main.ts DB/Redis password fingerprints, admin-files list ACL, collections static-route shadowing, spoofable post/comment likes, unbounded delayMs on `/rate-limit/stress-test`, donation donor_id spoof + PUT/DELETE IDOR + detail PII, task log-hours user_id spoof.

 * **Logging / i18n / Hebrew in API.** `challenges.controller.ts` class-validator messages and several controller comments remain Hebrew (violates repo English-only rule). Many controllers still use `console.error` instead of Nest `Logger`. Register default bio/city strings are Hebrew literals.

 * **Knowledge-link DELETE is a positive contrast** (in-handler admin role check). Notifications ownership and community-group-challenge JWT actor binding are the patterns to copy when fixing donor/task/follow/like spoofing.

 * **Prior audit PRs #105–#143** had not merged to `dev` as of this run; product HEAD is unchanged since 2026-08-20. Do not open a second product-fix PR from this report-only pass.

## 🚨 Critical Vulnerabilities

 * **Unauthenticated identifier-only account takeover via `POST /api/users/resolve-id`.** No `JwtAuthGuard`. Supplying a known `email`, `google_id`, or `firebase_uid` looks up `user_profiles`, optionally **links** a caller-supplied `firebase_uid`/`google_id` onto that row, then **mints a full JWT pair** (`accessToken` + `refreshToken`) for that user. If Admin SDK is initialized and no local row matches, `tryAutoCreateUserFromFirebase` calls `admin.auth().getUser(firebase_uid)` **without an ID token** and provisions a KC user + tokens from that UID. Concrete trigger: `POST /api/users/resolve-id` with `{ "email": "<victim>" }` returns tokens with the victim’s `roles`. SRS §2.1.6 currently documents this minting behavior — the spec itself should be tightened to require proof of possession (verified Firebase/Google ID token or existing session), not identifier matching. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (183–188) → `apps/api/src/services/user-auth.service.ts` (`resolveUserId` 270–342, `tryAutoCreateUserFromFirebase` 450–575, `linkResolveIdentifiers` 580–627, `buildResolveSuccessResponse` 645–672) -> Action: Remove token minting from resolve-id; require a verified ID token; never link identifiers from an unauthenticated body; bind any remaining resolve helper to `JwtAuthGuard`.

 * **Primary mobile login accepts email-only (password verification skipped, including empty string).** `POST /api/users/login` (`UsersController` / `UserAuthService.loginUser`) verifies argon2 **only when both** `loginData.password` and `user.password_hash` are truthy (223–231). Omitting `password`, sending `password: ""` (JS-falsy), or targeting a row with a null hash, falls through to `success: true` and returns email, phone, roles, `parent_manager_id`, and other profile fields. `/auth/login` correctly rejects missing hash and invalid passwords (528–541). Concrete trigger A: `POST /api/users/login` with `{ "email": "<victim>" }` (no password). Concrete trigger B: `{ "email": "<victim>", "password": "" }`. Combined with resolve-id, the documented mobile sequence (`apiService.loginUser` then `resolveUserId`) is an email-only takeover. Contrasts SRS §2.1.2 (`argon2.verify`). - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (116–118); `apps/api/src/services/user-auth.service.ts` (190–263, especially 223–231); contrast `apps/api/src/auth/controllers/auth.controller.ts` (501–541); mobile `apps/mobile/utils/apiService.ts` (463–467) / `dbConfig.ts` (`USER_LOGIN`) -> Action: Always require a non-empty password; fail closed if hash is missing; never return `success: true` without `argon2.verify`; stop treating resolve-id as a post-login token mint.

 * **Unauthenticated root-admin bootstrap via committed fallback email (plus identity-source split).** `KC_ROOT_ADMIN_EMAIL_FALLBACK` is committed in `kc-root-admin.defaults.ts`. Unauthenticated `POST /api/users/register` with that email sets `initialRoles = ["user", "admin"]` (101–102) when the row does not yet exist. Unauthenticated `POST /api/users/login` with the same email runs `array_append(roles, 'admin')` **before** the optional password check (212–220), then succeeds without a password (223–231). **Identity split:** `UserProfileService.getRootAdminEmail()` (52–56) reads only `ROOT_ADMIN_EMAIL` (empty string if unset) and is used solely to skip `roles` writes (349–361). The PUT role/hierarchy **block** (281–297) and unauthenticated `GET` listings compare a **hardcoded Gmail literal**, not the env var and not the shared constant. Concrete trigger A (empty DB / missing row): `POST /api/users/register` with the fallback email (password optional) creates an admin. Concrete trigger B (row exists): `POST /api/users/login` with `{ "email": "<fallback>" }` (no password) writes `admin` if missing, returns PII, then `POST /api/users/resolve-id` mints an admin JWT. Concrete trigger C (misconfiguration): setting `ROOT_ADMIN_EMAIL` to a different mailbox does **not** stop bootstrap of the fallback address and does **not** auto-promote the env mailbox. SRS §2.1.1 allows first-login promotion only as a bootstrap — not as an unauthenticated, passwordless grant. - File: `apps/api/src/config/kc-root-admin.defaults.ts`; `apps/api/src/services/user-auth.service.ts` (101–102, 212–231); `apps/api/src/services/user-profile.service.ts` (52–56, 281–297, 349–361); `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (111–118, 183–188) -> Action: Single source of truth for root-admin identity; require a verified secret/env match plus password (or disable public register for that email); never mutate roles before `argon2.verify`; bind bootstrap to a one-shot setup command, not a public API.

 * **Unauthenticated register binds attacker-chosen Firebase UID.** `POST /api/users/register` has no guard. `RegisterUserBody` accepts `firebase_uid` and `id`; the INSERT writes `userData.firebase_uid || userData.id || null` into `user_profiles.firebase_uid` (139). Password is optional (97–99). Concrete trigger: `POST /api/users/register` with `{ "email": "attacker@example.com", "firebase_uid": "<victim Firebase UID>" }` (or `{ "id": "<victim Firebase UID>" }`). The victim’s later Firebase ID-token path (`JwtAuthGuard` lookup by `firebase_uid`) attaches the attacker’s KC row. Complements resolve-id linking and profile `firebase_uid` mass-assignment as a **fourth** unauthenticated identity-binding path. - File: `apps/api/src/services/user-auth.service.ts` (`RegisterUserBody` 20–33, hash optional 97–99, bind at 139); `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (111–113) -> Action: Ignore client `firebase_uid`/`id` on register; require password; bind Firebase UID only after verified ID-token proof.

 * **Firebase JWKS fallback accepts any Firebase project.** When the Admin SDK is not initialized, `verifyIdTokenWithPublicJwks` takes `aud`/`iss` from the token and only compares them to `FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID` **if that env var is set**. File header states this is intentional. Concrete trigger: attacker creates their own Firebase project, obtains a valid Google-signed ID token, then (via unauthenticated `resolve-id` linking, register `firebase_uid` bind, or authenticated `PUT /api/users/:id` `firebase_uid` mass-assignment) binds that UID to a victim and presents the ID token to any `JwtAuthGuard` route. Complements resolve-id/mass-assignment with a **second login mechanism** that never mints a server JWT. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/services/firebase-admin.service.ts` (11–14, 150–185, 191–264); `apps/api/src/auth/guards/jwt-auth.guard.ts` (117–134, 170–213) -> Action: Require `FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID` (or service-account `project_id`) and reject tokens whose `aud` does not match; fail closed if unset in deployed environments.

 * **Google OAuth access-token fallback bypasses ID-token audience and email verification.** `POST /auth/google` verifies an ID token with `googleClient.verifyIdToken` and rejects unverified emails, but if verification fails **or** `idToken` is omitted, it falls back to `GET https://www.googleapis.com/oauth2/v3/userinfo` with a raw Bearer **access token** and hardcodes `emailVerified: true` (687–695). No `aud` binding to `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`. Concrete trigger: any Google OAuth access token (from an unrelated Google Cloud client) that can call userinfo mints a KC JWT. Third unauthenticated login path besides resolve-id and Firebase JWKS. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/controllers/auth.controller.ts` (613–698, especially 660–695) -> Action: Require a verified ID token with explicit `audience`; never accept access-token userinfo as a login proof; do not force `emailVerified: true`.

 * **Profile mass-assignment + password IDOR on `PUT /api/users/:id`.** `UpdateUserBody` accepts `password`, `roles`, `firebase_uid`, `parent_manager_id`, `hierarchy_level`. Controller uses `JwtAuthGuard` only — **no ownership check and no `req.user`**. Lookup is `id::text = $1 OR LOWER(email) = LOWER($1) OR firebase_uid = $1 OR google_id = $1` (267). Root-admin block (281–297) covers **only** `roles` / `parent_manager_id` / `hierarchy_level`; `password` is hashed and written (303–307) and `firebase_uid` is written (345–347) even for the root-admin row. `parent_manager_id`/`hierarchy_level` are on the DTO and blocked for root-admin but **not written** to SQL for other users. Concrete trigger: any JWT (including one minted by resolve-id) `PUT /api/users/<victim-uuid-or-email>` with `{ "password": "...", "roles": ["admin"], "firebase_uid": "<attacker>" }` resets the victim’s password, escalates roles (except the hardcoded root-admin email’s roles), and hijacks Firebase identity. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (126–133); `apps/api/src/services/user-profile.service.ts` (`UpdateUserBody` 18–32, lookup 267, root-admin block 281–297, password 303–307, firebase_uid/roles 345–361) -> Action: Bind updates to `req.user.userId`; reject `roles`/`firebase_uid` except via admin-only endpoints; require current password (or admin) to change password; do not resolve PUT targets by email/firebase_uid/google_id.

 * **JWT-gated donation create spoofs `donor_id` and can inflate another user’s donation totals (new 2026-08-28).** `POST /api/donations` uses `JwtAuthGuard` but never reads `req.user`. INSERT uses `donationData.donor_id` (503). When `type === "money"` and `amount` is set, a second statement does `total_donations_amount = total_donations_amount + $1` for that `donor_id` (525–534). Controller TODOs at 481–484 already note missing authorization. Concrete trigger: any JWT `POST /api/donations` with `{ "donor_id": "<victim-uuid>", "type": "money", "amount": 999999, "title": "...", "category_id": "<id>" }` attributes a donation to the victim and increases their public donation total. Complements unauthenticated `GET /api/stats/analytics/categories` money SUM. Contrasts `ChallengesController` / `CommunityGroupChallengesController`, which bind actor from `CurrentUser`. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/donations.controller.ts` (478–534) -> Action: Set `donor_id` exclusively from `req.user.userId`; ignore client donor/recipient identity fields except where an admin flow is explicitly authorized.

 * **JWT-gated donation PUT/DELETE is an unscoped IDOR (new 2026-08-28).** `PUT /api/donations/:id` (698–744) and `DELETE /api/donations/:id` (746+) use `JwtAuthGuard` only. Update applies `title`, `description`, `amount`, `status`, images, tags, metadata with **no** `donor_id` / `req.user` comparison. Delete loads `id, status` then proceeds without an owner or admin check. Concrete trigger: any JWT `PUT /api/donations/<id>` with `{ "status": "cancelled", "amount": 0 }` or `DELETE /api/donations/<id>` alters or removes another user’s donation. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/donations.controller.ts` (698–746) -> Action: Require `req.user.userId === donor_id` or `AdminAuthGuard`; do not accept client `status`/`amount` on a generic update without an explicit admin/owner policy.

 * **Unauthenticated donation-detail PII (new 2026-08-28).** `GET /api/donations/:id` has no guard. The SELECT joins `user_profiles` and returns `up.phone as donor_phone, up.email as donor_email` (676–685) in addition to donation row fields (`d.*`). List `GET /api/donations` is also unauthenticated but only exposes donor name/city/avatar (596–598). Concrete trigger: `GET /api/donations/<uuid>` returns that donor’s phone and email (cached 15 minutes under `donation_<id>`). Violates SRS §3.5 data minimization. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/donations.controller.ts` (667–696) -> Action: Auth-gate detail; never project `phone`/`email` to non-self, non-admin, non-operator callers; strip those columns from the public cache payload.

 * **Unauthenticated user directory and PII.** `GET /api/users` and `GET /api/users/:id` have no guards. `getUserById` SELECTs `email` and `phone` and matches UUID **or** email / `firebase_uid` / `google_id` (126–157). `getUsers` SELECTs `u.email` (515) plus roles, manager email, and hierarchy. Also unauthenticated: `GET search` (returns `id, name, email, avatar_url, roles` for `is_active` users), `GET :id/activities`, `GET :id/stats`, `GET stats/summary`. Concrete trigger: `GET /api/users` dumps the directory; `GET /api/users/<email>` returns that user’s phone; `GET /api/users/search?q=ab` enumerates emails. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (40–43, 101–163, 121–123, 135–149); `apps/api/src/services/user-profile.service.ts` (search 68–77, getUserById ~126–157, getUsers ~515) -> Action: Require auth; redact phone/email/roles for non-self, non-admin callers; stop identifier-OR lookup on a public GET.

 * **Client-supplied hierarchy actors skip authorization when omitted or not found.** `setManagerEnsureRequesterAdminIfProvided` returns `null` (allow) when `requestingUserId` is omitted **or** the user row is not found (492–517). Promote and `manageHierarchy` trust body admin/manager IDs with no JWT binding. `GET eligible-for-promotion/:adminId`, `GET hierarchy/tree`, and `GET :id/hierarchy` have **no** `UseGuards`. Concrete trigger: omit `requestingAdminId` / `requestingUserId` and mutate hierarchy; or scrape the full tree unauthenticated. - File: `apps/api/src/services/user-hierarchy.service.ts` (492–517, 871–914, 945–980); `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (91–108) -> Action: Require `JwtAuthGuard` + `AdminAuthGuard`; bind actor exclusively from `req.user.userId`; fail closed if the actor is missing.

 * **Spoofable follow graph.** `POST/DELETE /api/users/:id/follow` are JWT-gated but `UserFollowService` inserts/deletes using body `follower_id`, not `req.user.userId`. Concrete trigger: authenticated user A follows/unfollows as user B. - File: `apps/api/src/services/user-follow.service.ts` (body `follower_id`); `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (165–180) -> Action: Bind `follower_id` exclusively from the JWT.

 * **Unauthenticated chat writes impersonate `sender_id`.** Class-level `OptionalAuthGuard` always returns `true` (338). Unauthenticated `POST /api/chat/messages` uses body `sender_id` (456–464). Authenticated callers that send a mismatched `sender_id` are rejected (450–455); guests are not. Mark-read falls back to `body.user_id`. Concrete trigger: `POST /api/chat/messages` with `{ "sender_id": "<victim>", "content": "..." }` (and a conversation id / participants) inserts a message attributed to the victim. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/guards/jwt-auth.guard.ts` (308–339); `apps/api/src/controllers/chat.controller.ts` (46, 441–465, 679, 759) -> Action: Require `JwtAuthGuard` on write (and preferably read) paths; never accept client `sender_id`.

 * **Unguarded mutating APIs (rides, items, CRM, community-members, collections, dedicated-items, rate-limit).** No `UseGuards` on `RidesController` (book spoofs `passenger_id` 374–468), `ItemsDeliveryController` (create with caller `owner_id`), `DedicatedItemsController`, `CrmController`, `CommunityMembersController`, `ItemsController` collections CRUD (`POST/PUT/DELETE` 80–108; allowlist includes `users`, `chats`, `messages`, `tasks`, `notifications`, `rides`, `organizations`), and `RateLimitController`. Redis-test (get/set/delete/keys) is registered **only when `NODE_ENV !== production`** (`app.module.ts` 122) — Railway `railway.toml.dev` uses `NODE_ENV=development`, so it is live on that service. Concrete trigger: unauthenticated `POST /api/rides/:id/book` with another user’s `passenger_id`. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/rides.controller.ts`; `items-delivery.controller.ts`; `apps/api/src/items/dedicated-items.controller.ts`; `crm.controller.ts`; `community-members.controller.ts`; `apps/api/src/items/items.controller.ts`; `rate-limit.controller.ts`; `redis-test.controller.ts`; `app.module.ts` (122) -> Action: `JwtAuthGuard` (admin where appropriate) on all mutating routes; bind actor IDs from JWT; remove redis-test and rate-limit demo routes from deployed environments.

 * **JwtAuthGuard rate-limit key is the shared HS256 header prefix — and unauthenticated `/rate-limit/custom` can plant a platform-wide block.** `checkRateLimit(token.substring(0, 16), "api_access")` (96–114). Issued header is always `{alg:HS256,typ:JWT}`, so the first 16 chars are always `eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1`. 100 req/60s then 5-minute block is **global for all JWT users**. Thrown as `UnauthorizedException` (401). Mobile `authFetchInterceptor` treats 401 as logout (75–89). `DELETE /rate-limit/clear` already deletes both window and block keys for an attacker-chosen identifier. `POST /rate-limit/custom` has no guard and passes client `rule` + `customKey` into `applyCustomRateLimit` (`rate-limit.controller.ts` 233–264; `rate-limit.service.ts` 269–276). Redis keys are `blocked:{ruleType}:{identifier}`. Concrete trigger: two unauthenticated `POST /rate-limit/custom` bodies `{ "identifier": "eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1", "customKey": "api_access", "rule": { "requests": 1, "windowMs": 1000, "blockDurationMs": 86400000 } }` write `blocked:api_access:eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1` for 24 hours; every subsequent `JwtAuthGuard` request 401s and mobile clients clear session. Complementary clear: `DELETE /rate-limit/clear` with `{ "identifier": "eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1", "ruleType": "api_access" }` lifts the same bucket. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/guards/jwt-auth.guard.ts` (96–114); `apps/api/src/auth/services/jwt.service.ts` (276–281); `apps/api/src/auth/services/rate-limit.service.ts` (82–141, 181–192, 269–276); `apps/api/src/controllers/rate-limit.controller.ts` (155–167, 233–264); `apps/mobile/auth/interceptors/authFetchInterceptor.ts` (46–89) -> Action: Rate-limit by `userId` (after verify) or a hash of the full token; return 429 not 401; remove `/rate-limit/*` demo routes from deployed environments (or `AdminAuthGuard` + no client-supplied `customKey`/`rule`).

 * **Admin-files list ACL is JWT-only.** `GET /api/admin-files` and `GET folders` use `JwtAuthGuard`; POST/DELETE correctly use `AdminAuthGuard`. Concrete trigger: any authenticated user lists Finance/HR file URLs. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/admin-files.controller.ts` (80–140 vs 142–203) -> Action: Use `AdminAuthGuard` on GET/folders as well.

 * **Unauthenticated donation analytics and real-time presence.** `GET /api/stats/analytics/categories` returns donation money SUM with no guard (439–496). `GET /api/stats/real-time` returns `users_online` and last-hour `user_activities` (782–831). `analytics/users` correctly uses `AdminAuthGuard`. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/stats.controller.ts` (439–496, 782–831) -> Action: Auth-gate (admin for money totals); do not expose presence without auth.

 * **Demo session controller: unauthenticated session enumeration and forced logout.** Demo `SessionController` (`/session`) has no guards. `POST /session/login` verifies real `password_hash` via argon2. `GET /session/user/:userId` lists sessions; `DELETE /session/logout/:sessionId` and `DELETE /session/logout-all/:userId` destroy sessions without proving possession. Concrete trigger: `DELETE /session/logout-all/<victim-uuid>` signs the victim out of all devices. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/controllers/session.controller.ts` (27–229) -> Action: Remove from production/dev deployments; if kept for tests, bind to `JwtAuthGuard` + ownership and disable login.

 * **Unauthenticated Google Places proxy and unencoded `place_id`.** `GET /autocomplete`, `/place-details`, `/search-stats` have no guards and spend `GOOGLE_API_KEY`. `/place-details` interpolates `place_id` without `encodeURIComponent` (line 75), so `&`-bearing values append extra query parameters to Google’s API. `/search-stats` enumerates Redis `search_activity:*` / `places:autocomplete:*` keys. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/places.controller.ts` (9–103) -> Action: Auth + throttle; encode `place_id`; hide search-stats behind admin.

 * **Request-path and startup `DROP TABLE ... CASCADE`.** `PostsController.migrateExistingPostsTableColumns` / `ensurePostsTable` can `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS posts CASCADE` on a live request if `id`/`author_id` are missing (line 78). Related drops: `post_likes` (309), `comment_likes` + `post_comments` (373–374). `tasks-schema.service.ts:41` drops `posts` via task side-effects. `DatabaseInit` drops legacy JSONB `donations` (568) and `rides` (670) on `onModuleInit` when a `data` column is detected. Concrete trigger: a schema-compat mismatch (or a poisoned `information_schema` view in a compromised DB role) destroys posts/likes/comments and their FKs during ordinary traffic or process boot. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/posts.controller.ts` (78, 309, 373–374); `apps/api/src/tasks/tasks-schema.service.ts` (41); `apps/api/src/database/database.init.ts` (568, 670) -> Action: Never DROP on the request path; replace with expand-only migrations; gate startup drops behind an explicit one-shot env flag.

 * **Committed credentials and PII backups in git.** `apps/api/railway.toml.dev` hardcodes a development `JWT_SECRET` (line 34). `docs/SSOT/runbooks/api/environment-separation.md` embeds a production `JWT_SECRET` (line 126; line 102 is a placeholder). Values are not repeated here. `apps/api/src/main.ts` (149–218) fingerprints environments by matching **database password and Redis password/host substrings** committed in source — rotate those credentials and replace the check with an explicit `ENVIRONMENT` flag. 49 tracked JSON files under `apps/api/data-backups/20251224-162421/` include `user_profiles.json` whose keys are `email`, `phone`, and `password_hash`. The env cross-check does not prevent these files from shipping. - File: `apps/api/railway.toml.dev:34`; `docs/SSOT/runbooks/api/environment-separation.md:126`; `apps/api/src/main.ts` (149–218); `apps/api/data-backups/20251224-162421/*` -> Action: Rotate all exposed JWT secrets and DB/Redis credentials whose fragments appear in git; purge secrets from git history; gitignore backups; use secret managers only.

 * **JWT-gated but unscoped task list.** `GET /api/tasks` uses `JwtAuthGuard` then lists by query filters (`assignee`, `status`, …) with **no** restriction to the caller’s tasks. Any authenticated user can enumerate all tasks (ClosedRoute even queries another profile’s `assignee`). - File: `apps/api/src/tasks/tasks.controller.ts` (74–146) -> Action: Default-scope to `req.user`; require admin for global lists.

 * **Unthrottled primary mobile login/register.** SRS §2.1.2 and §3.1 require `ThrottlerGuard` on auth. `AuthController` (`/auth/*`) has class-level `ThrottlerGuard`, but the **primary mobile path** (`apiService.ts` / `dbConfig.USER_LOGIN`) calls `POST /api/users/login` and `POST /api/users/register` on `UsersController`, which have **no** throttle and no global `APP_GUARD`. Concrete trigger: unbounded credential stuffing against `/api/users/login` (and account-creation spam on `/api/users/register`) bypasses the documented 60 req/60s auth limit. Magnified by the password-skip finding: stuffing is unnecessary if email alone (or `password: ""`) succeeds. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (111–118); `apps/mobile/utils/apiService.ts` (463–467); `apps/mobile/utils/dbConfig.ts`; `apps/api/src/app.module.ts` (72–77) -> Action: Apply `ThrottlerGuard` (stricter login tier) to `/api/users/login` and `/register`; register global `APP_GUARD`; keep `/auth/*` and `/api/users/*` auth surfaces consistent.

 * **JWT refresh persists stale roles.** SRS §2.1.4 requires refresh to **fetch latest roles from the database** so demotions propagate. `JwtService.refreshAccessToken` copies `payload.roles` from the presented refresh token and issues a new access token with those roles; there is no `user_profiles` lookup. Redis existence of the refresh token **is** checked inside `verifyToken` (123–129). HMAC compare uses `!==` rather than `timingSafeEqual` (308). Concrete trigger: an admin is demoted to `user` while still holding a valid 30-day refresh token; `POST /auth/refresh` continues to mint access tokens with `roles: ["admin"]` until that refresh token expires or is revoked in Redis. Complements profile mass-assignment (attacker can also *gain* roles) by letting a formerly privileged user *keep* them after a legitimate demotion. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/services/jwt.service.ts` (146–177, especially 157–165, compare at 308); `apps/api/src/auth/controllers/auth.controller.ts` (981–998) -> Action: On refresh, load `roles` from `user_profiles` by `payload.userId`; reject refresh if the user is inactive; use `crypto.timingSafeEqual`; consider rotating the refresh token.

---

## 🐞 Bugs & Logic Issues

 * **JWT-gated task hour log spoofs body `user_id` (new 2026-08-28).** `POST /api/tasks/:id/log-hours` uses `JwtAuthGuard` but never reads `req.user`. It resolves body `user_id` to a UUID and UPSERTs `task_time_logs.actual_hours` for that user (`ON CONFLICT (task_id, user_id) DO UPDATE`). Concrete trigger: any JWT `POST /api/tasks/<id>/log-hours` with `{ "hours": 8, "user_id": "<victim-uuid>" }` overwrites the victim’s logged hours (or inserts a row attributed to them). - Recommendation: Bind `user_id` exclusively from the JWT; ignore body actor fields. File: `apps/api/src/tasks/tasks.controller.ts` (384–445).

 * **Spoofable post comment likes (new 2026-08-28, same family as 08-27 post likes).** `POST /api/posts/:postId/comments/:commentId/like` is JWT-gated but reads `body.user_id` exclusively (2163–2209); `req.user.userId` is never consulted. Concrete trigger: any JWT with `{ "user_id": "<victim-uuid>" }` toggles the victim’s comment like and can notify the comment author as that user. - Recommendation: Bind `user_id` exclusively from the JWT. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/posts.controller.ts` (2152–2217).

 * **Unauthenticated rate-limit stress-test holds connections for attacker-chosen `delayMs`.** `POST /rate-limit/stress-test` has no guard. `requests` is capped at 50, but `delayMs` is used as `body.delayMs || 100` with **no upper bound** (`rate-limit.controller.ts` 61–97). The loop `await`s `setTimeout(delayMs)` between iterations, so one request can pin an HTTP connection until `httpServer.setTimeout(30000)` (`main.ts` 441–443) kills it. Concrete trigger: parallel `POST /rate-limit/stress-test` with `{ "requests": 50, "delayMs": 60000 }` (effective hold ~30s each) plus Redis writes via `checkRateLimit`. Combined with `/rate-limit/custom` this is a cheap unauthenticated DoS / Redis-amplification surface. - Recommendation: Remove the controller from deployed environments; if kept, `AdminAuthGuard`, cap `delayMs` (e.g. ≤ 20), and do not accept client `identifier`/`ruleType`/`customKey`.

 * **Spoofable post likes.** `POST /api/posts/:postId/like` is JWT-gated but reads `body.user_id` exclusively (1345–1406); `req.user.userId` is never consulted. Like/unlike and `notifyAuthorOfNewPostLike` therefore run as any UUID the caller supplies. Same actor-binding gap as comments. Concrete trigger: any JWT `POST /api/posts/<id>/like` with `{ "user_id": "<victim-uuid>" }` toggles the victim’s like and can notify the author as that user. - Recommendation: Bind `user_id` exclusively from the JWT; ignore body actor fields. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/posts.controller.ts` (1343–1406).

 * **Ride booking approval can oversell seats.** `updateBookingStatus` sets status to `approved` then only marks the ride `full` when remaining ≤ 0; no capacity reject and no `FOR UPDATE` row lock before approve. Concurrent approvals can exceed `available_seats`. Booking create does check remaining seats (407–410) but that check is not applied on approve. - Recommendation: In one transaction, lock the ride + sum approved seats; reject approve when `seats_requested` would exceed remaining capacity. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/rides.controller.ts` (505–555).

 * **Unauthenticated real-time stats cartesian join.** `GET /api/stats/real-time` has no guard (already critical-adjacent for `users_online` + last-hour activity). The donations/rides subquery is `FROM donations d FULL OUTER JOIN rides r ON 1=1`, a full cartesian product. Concrete trigger: with D donation rows and R ride rows, Postgres materializes D×R rows before `COUNT(CASE WHEN d.status = 'active')`, so `active_donations` is inflated by ~R and `active_rides` by ~D. As tables grow this is also an unauthenticated CPU/memory DoS. - Recommendation: Replace with two independent `COUNT(*)` queries (or `UNION ALL` of aggregates); auth-gate the endpoint. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/stats.controller.ts` (782–831, join at 803–809).

 * **Collections static GET routes are shadowed.** `ItemsController` registers parameterized `GET :collection` (line 43) **before** static `GET popular-collections` (136) and `GET cache-stats` (153). Nest matches the first compatible 1-segment GET, so those helpers run `validateCollection` against the path name and return `400 Invalid collection` (`popular-collections` / `cache-stats` are not in `ALLOWED_COLLECTIONS`). `GET user-activity/:userId` is 2-segment and still reachable. - Recommendation: Declare static paths before `:collection`; or nest helpers under a non-colliding prefix. File: `apps/api/src/items/items.controller.ts` (43–77 vs 136–167).

 * **Task done PATCH can create duplicate completion posts.** `evaluateMarkDoneTimeLogRequirement` skips when `oldStatus === "done"`, but `runUpdateTaskCompletionPosts` still runs whenever `body.status === "done"` (`tasks-patch-mutation.service.ts:380–381`) with no transition check. `finalizePerformerCompletionPost` INSERTs a new `task_completion` row when no matching `task_assignment` title is updated, so a second PATCH to `done` duplicates the feed post. - Recommendation: Gate completion side effects on `oldStatus !== "done"`; add an idempotent unique constraint / upsert for completion posts.

 * **Auth refresh waiters hang on failure.** `apps/mobile/auth/interceptors/authFetchInterceptor.ts` (46–89): failed refresh clears `refreshSubscribers` without resolving waiters subscribed via `subscribeTokenRefresh`. Parallel 401s wait forever. Combined with the JwtAuthGuard 401-on-quota finding, a shared rate-limit 401 can both log out the initiating client and hang sibling in-flight requests. - Recommendation: Reject/resolve all subscribers on failure paths before clearing tokens; do not treat 429/quota as auth failure.

 * **ClosedRoute task type inconsistency.** `ClosedRoute.tsx` filters existing content with `type === 'task'` (~324) but pushes `type: 'task_post'` (~351), so duplicate suppression against post-derived tasks is ineffective. - Recommendation: Align filter and push types (or normalize via a single type constant matching `FeedItem.type`).

 * **Spoofable post comment author.** `POST /api/posts/:postId/comments` is JWT-gated but uses body `user_id`, not `req.user.userId`. Any authenticated user can attribute comments to another account. - Recommendation: Bind `user_id` exclusively from the JWT. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/posts.controller.ts` (1573–1584).

 * **Unauthenticated knowledge-link writes.** `POST /api/donations/knowledge/links` is explicitly public and inserts caller-supplied URL/display name (optional spoofed `createdByUserId`). Spam/defacement surface. DELETE is JWT-gated and **does** check `admin` / `super_admin` / `org_admin` in-handler (312–318) — decorator is JWT-only. - Recommendation: Require auth; rate-limit; ignore client `createdByUserId`. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/donations.controller.ts` (226–291, 304–318).

 * **`PUT /api/posts/:postId` owner check falls back to body `user_id`.** Guard sets `req.user.userId` (JWT payload), so the fallback is currently dead in the happy path; if the payload shape ever omitted `userId`/`id`/`sub`, a caller could pass another user’s id and pass the `author_id` check. Same fallback exists on related post mutate handlers (~2445, ~2531). - Recommendation: Remove `body.user_id` fallback; compare `req.user.userId` only. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/posts.controller.ts` (2283–2332). (The 2026-05-04 `images` TEXT[] mapping in commit `073722f` is otherwise correct.)

 * **Posts / tasks / dedicated-items catch blocks leak `error.message` to clients.** Contrasts CODE_QUALITY `constraints.md` (never send stack/driver text to clients). Firebase verify also returns `error.message` (`jwt-auth.guard.ts` 211–213). - Recommendation: Map to stable error codes; log details server-side only.

 * **Register/login write Hebrew UI strings into stored profile defaults** (`bio`, `city`) in `user-auth.service.ts` (120–126, 500, 505). Violates English-only code/data defaults; i18n should live in the client. - Recommendation: Store locale-neutral defaults; let the client localize empty states.

 * **`SYNC_API_KEY` defaults to `change-me-in-production`.** Sync mutate routes also use `AdminAuthGuard`, so this is not a standalone bypass today, but a missing env var leaves a known default and the extra key check is skipped when the default is still in place (`sync.controller.ts` 422, 511). - Recommendation: Fail closed if unset in production. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/sync.controller.ts` (27–40, 422–424, 511–513).

 * **Firebase JWKS fabricates `exp` when the claim is missing.** After `jose.jwtVerify`, `verifyIdTokenWithPublicJwks` sets `exp` to `now+3600` if `payload.exp` is not a number (216–219). `jwtVerify` is not passed `requiredClaims: ['exp']`. Google-issued Firebase ID tokens include `exp`, so this is defense-in-depth, not a current Google-token bypass. - Recommendation: Require `exp` (and reject missing/non-numeric); never invent expiry. File: `apps/api/src/auth/services/firebase-admin.service.ts` (191–236).

 * **Custom JWT HMAC compare is not constant-time; missing `exp` is treated as not expired.** `verifyTokenSignature` uses `signature !== expectedSignature` (`jwt.service.ts:308`) instead of `crypto.timingSafeEqual`. `verifyToken` then does `if (payload.exp < now)` (119); a forged payload without `exp` yields `undefined < number` → `false`, so expiry is skipped. Forgery still requires `JWT_SECRET` (which is committed for the Railway dev service). - Recommendation: Decode both signature sides to equal-length buffers and `timingSafeEqual`; reject missing/non-numeric `exp` before compare. File: `apps/api/src/auth/services/jwt.service.ts` (107–121, 294–316).

 * **Email enumeration on the primary login path.** `POST /api/users/login` returns `"User not found"` vs `"Invalid password"` (206–229). `/auth/login` correctly uses a generic `"Invalid email or password"`. Combined with password-skip, a missing or empty password on a known email is already a positive existence oracle. - Recommendation: Use a single generic failure for both missing user and bad password; still require `argon2.verify`.

 * **`is_active` is not enforced on authentication (defense-in-depth).** `UserProfileService.searchUsers` filters `is_active = true` (73), and stats count active users, but `loginUser`, `/auth/login`, `resolveUserId` / JWT mint, and `JwtAuthGuard` never reject `is_active = false`. There is no public API to flip the flag (`UpdateUserBody` omits it), so the realistic trigger is a direct SQL/admin update. Once flipped, the user disappears from search but can still log in (including via the passwordless `/api/users/login` path) and mint tokens. - Recommendation: Fail closed on `is_active !== true` in login, resolve-id, refresh, and `JwtAuthGuard`; add an admin-only deactivate endpoint if product needs it.

## 🏗️ Architecture & Clean Code

 * **No Domain / Application / Infrastructure folders exist** under `apps/` or `packages/` (count 0). Target in `docs/SSOT/CODE_QUALITY/layers-and-boundaries.md` is Infrastructure → Application → Domain (inward). Closest pure islands: `apps/api/src/tasks/task-completion-participants.ts`, `tasks-validation.ts`, `tasks-query-params.ts`, `tasks.types.ts` (no Nest/pg imports). `packages/` still only ships `config-eslint`.

 * **Fat Nest controllers/services own SQL + HTTP + DDL.** Worst offenders (LOC): `posts.controller.ts` (2741) — Pool + Redis + `DROP TABLE`; `stats.controller.ts` (1709); `user-hierarchy.service.ts` (1675) — fat *service* with the same SQL+policy mix; `database.init.ts` (1374); `community-group-challenges.controller.ts` (1097); `donations.controller.ts` (892); `chat.controller.ts` (813); `rides.controller.ts` (779). `items-delivery.service.ts` is misplaced under `controllers/`. SRS §5.2 already notes donations/rides logic in controllers. Today’s new donation IDOR / `donor_id` spoof lives in that fat controller — actor binding is inconsistent with JWT-scoped challenge controllers.

 * **Better-shaped but still not Clean Architecture:** `users.controller.ts` is thin and delegates to services — those services still inject `pg.Pool`. `items/` controller → `ItemsService`. `tasks/` is split into services; the controller still injects Pool for some paths (including unscoped `log-hours`). Positive contrasts: `CommunityGroupChallengesController` binds creator from JWT; `ChallengesController` scopes to `CurrentUser`; `NotificationsController.validateOwnership` binds URL `userId` to `req.user`.

 * **Operator matching module still missing** (SRS §2.14 / §5.1 / §10.1): no `/api/operator/*`, no matching tables, no mobile Shiduchim Tov screens. `OperatorAuthGuard` exists in `jwt-auth.guard.ts` but has no consumers. Anonymity column still absent from posts DDL.

 * **Mobile debt hubs:** `utils/databaseService.ts` (1225), `apiService.ts` (1055), `enhancedDatabaseService.ts` (897); duplicate `google_auth/SecureApiService.ts` (1165). Duplicate mappers `mapPostToFeedItemForItemsScreen` / `ForTrumpScreen` vs `useFeedData` (logged as follow-ups in `tech-debt-log.md`). Largest tracked source: `apps/mobile/utils/bubbles_Data.tsx` (4852). **195** source `.ts`/`.tsx` files >200 LOC (apps+packages, excluding node_modules/dist/spec/test).

 * **ThrottlerModule is configured but not registered as `APP_GUARD`** (`app.module.ts` 72–77). Only `AuthController` and `NotificationsController` opt in. Global 60 req/60s (SRS §3.1) is therefore not actually global. Confirmed: zero `APP_GUARD` matches under `apps/api/src`.

 * **Custom HMAC JWT** (SRS §10.3 Medium): hand-rolled in `jwt.service.ts` rather than `jose`/`jsonwebtoken`. Functional, but `alg` is never pinned on verify (signature is always HMAC over the provided header, so `alg=none` does not skip verify — defense-in-depth still missing); compare is not constant-time; missing `exp` is not rejected.

 * **SRS / code mismatches that remain open.** §2.1.4 (refresh must reload DB roles) is unimplemented. §2.1.2 / §3.1 (`ThrottlerGuard` on login; `argon2.verify`; global 60/60) apply only to `/auth/*` + notifications, not `/api/users/login` (which also skips the password check, including `password: ""`, and auto-promotes the hardcoded root-admin email). §2.1.3 requires Google ID-token verification; access-token userinfo fallback contradicts it. §3.2 lists `OptionalAuthGuard` as “enrich unauthenticated requests”; implementation always allows and chat writes as `sender_id`. §3.2 also says admin access is role-based only with no hardcoded emails — the PUT root-admin freeze still uses a Gmail literal. §3.5 data minimization is violated by unauthenticated user/email/phone, **donation-detail phone/email**, and admin-file list. §10.1 operator matching module / `/api/operator/*` / matching tables still missing (`OperatorAuthGuard` exists unused).

New first-class findings: (1) unauthenticated `POST /rate-limit/custom` can **install** a Redis block on JwtAuthGuard’s shared HS256-header identifier (`customKey: "api_access"`) with attacker-chosen `blockDurationMs`, forcing platform-wide 401 and mobile logout — complementary to the already-known unauthenticated `DELETE /rate-limit/clear` which only *clears* that bucket; (2) unauthenticated `POST /rate-limit/stress-test` accepts unbounded `delayMs` (capped in practice by the 30s HTTP server timeout); (3) JWT-gated `POST /api/posts/:postId/like` spoofs `body.user_id` (same actor-binding gap as comments). npm audit totals again non-deterministic (**44 → 94 → 95**; critical still **4**: `handlebars`, `shell-quote`, `tar`, `websocket-driver`). Files >200 LOC: **197**. Request-path XSS/SQLi patterns remain absent; rides/items-delivery `ORDER BY` columns are allowlisted.
