## 🚨 Critical Vulnerabilities

 * **Unauthenticated identifier-only account takeover via `POST /api/users/resolve-id`.** No `JwtAuthGuard`. Supplying a known `email`, `google_id`, or `firebase_uid` looks up `user_profiles`, optionally **links** a caller-supplied `firebase_uid`/`google_id` onto that row, then **mints a full JWT pair** (`accessToken` + `refreshToken`) for that user. If Admin SDK is initialized and no local row matches, `tryAutoCreateUserFromFirebase` calls `admin.auth().getUser(firebase_uid)` **without an ID token** and provisions a KC user + tokens from that UID. Concrete trigger: `POST /api/users/resolve-id` with `{ "email": "<victim>" }` returns tokens with the victim’s `roles`. SRS §2.1.6 currently documents this minting behavior — the spec itself should be tightened to require proof of possession (verified Firebase/Google ID token or existing session), not identifier matching. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (183–188) → `apps/api/src/services/user-auth.service.ts` (`resolveUserId` 270–342, `tryAutoCreateUserFromFirebase` 450–575, `linkResolveIdentifiers` 580–627, `buildResolveSuccessResponse` 645–672) -> Action: Remove token minting from resolve-id; require a verified ID token; never link identifiers from an unauthenticated body; bind any remaining resolve helper to `JwtAuthGuard`.

 * **Primary mobile login accepts email-only (password verification skipped).** `POST /api/users/login` (`UsersController` / `UserAuthService.loginUser`) verifies argon2 **only when both** `loginData.password` and `user.password_hash` are truthy (223–231). Omitting `password`, or targeting a row with a null hash, falls through to `success: true` and returns email, phone, roles, `parent_manager_id`, and other profile fields. `/auth/login` correctly rejects missing hash and invalid passwords (528–541). Concrete trigger: `POST /api/users/login` with `{ "email": "<victim>" }` (no password). Combined with resolve-id, the documented mobile sequence (`apiService.loginUser` then `resolveUserId`) is an email-only takeover. Contrasts SRS §2.1.2 (`argon2.verify`). - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (116–118); `apps/api/src/services/user-auth.service.ts` (190–263, especially 223–231); contrast `apps/api/src/auth/controllers/auth.controller.ts` (501–541); mobile `apps/mobile/utils/apiService.ts` (463–467) / `dbConfig.ts` (`USER_LOGIN`) -> Action: Always require a password; fail closed if hash is missing; never return `success: true` without `argon2.verify`; stop treating resolve-id as a post-login token mint.

 * **Unauthenticated root-admin bootstrap via committed fallback email (plus identity-source split, new 2026-08-25).** `KC_ROOT_ADMIN_EMAIL_FALLBACK` is committed in `kc-root-admin.defaults.ts`. Unauthenticated `POST /api/users/register` with that email sets `initialRoles = ["user", "admin"]` (101–102) when the row does not yet exist. Unauthenticated `POST /api/users/login` with the same email runs `array_append(roles, 'admin')` **before** the optional password check (212–220), then succeeds without a password (223–231). **Identity split:** `UserProfileService.getRootAdminEmail()` (52–56) reads only `ROOT_ADMIN_EMAIL` (empty string if unset) and is used solely to skip `roles` writes (349–361). The PUT role/hierarchy **block** (281–297) and unauthenticated `GET` listings (134, 181, 519, 566) compare a **hardcoded Gmail literal**, not the env var and not the shared constant. Concrete trigger A (empty DB / missing row): `POST /api/users/register` with the fallback email (password optional) creates an admin. Concrete trigger B (row exists): `POST /api/users/login` with `{ "email": "<fallback>" }` (no password) writes `admin` if missing, returns PII, then `POST /api/users/resolve-id` mints an admin JWT. Concrete trigger C (misconfiguration): setting `ROOT_ADMIN_EMAIL` to a different mailbox does **not** stop bootstrap of the fallback address and does **not** auto-promote the env mailbox. SRS §2.1.1 allows first-login promotion only as a bootstrap — not as an unauthenticated, passwordless grant. - File: `apps/api/src/config/kc-root-admin.defaults.ts`; `apps/api/src/services/user-auth.service.ts` (101–102, 212–231); `apps/api/src/services/user-profile.service.ts` (52–56, 281–297, 349–361); `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (111–118, 183–188) -> Action: Single source of truth for root-admin identity; require a verified secret/env match plus password (or disable public register for that email); never mutate roles before `argon2.verify`; bind bootstrap to a one-shot setup command, not a public API.

 * **Unauthenticated register binds attacker-chosen Firebase UID.** `POST /api/users/register` has no guard. `RegisterUserBody` accepts `firebase_uid` and `id`; the INSERT writes `userData.firebase_uid || userData.id || null` into `user_profiles.firebase_uid` (139). Password is optional (97–99). Concrete trigger: `POST /api/users/register` with `{ "email": "attacker@example.com", "firebase_uid": "<victim Firebase UID>" }` (or `{ "id": "<victim Firebase UID>" }`). The victim’s later Firebase ID-token path (`JwtAuthGuard` lookup by `firebase_uid`) attaches the attacker’s KC row. Complements resolve-id linking and profile `firebase_uid` mass-assignment as a **fourth** unauthenticated identity-binding path. - File: `apps/api/src/services/user-auth.service.ts` (`RegisterUserBody` 20–33, hash optional 97–99, bind at 139); `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (111–113) -> Action: Ignore client `firebase_uid`/`id` on register; require password; bind Firebase UID only after verified ID-token proof.

 * **Firebase JWKS fallback accepts any Firebase project.** When the Admin SDK is not initialized, `verifyIdTokenWithPublicJwks` takes `aud`/`iss` from the token and only compares them to `FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID` **if that env var is set**. File header states this is intentional. Concrete trigger: attacker creates their own Firebase project, obtains a valid Google-signed ID token, then (via unauthenticated `resolve-id` linking, register `firebase_uid` bind, or authenticated `PUT /api/users/:id` `firebase_uid` mass-assignment) binds that UID to a victim and presents the ID token to any `JwtAuthGuard` route. Complements resolve-id/mass-assignment with a **second login mechanism** that never mints a server JWT. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/services/firebase-admin.service.ts` (11–14, 150–185, 191–264); `apps/api/src/auth/guards/jwt-auth.guard.ts` (117–134, 170–213) -> Action: Require `FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID` (or service-account `project_id`) and reject tokens whose `aud` does not match; fail closed if unset in deployed environments.

 * **Google OAuth access-token fallback bypasses ID-token audience and email verification.** `POST /auth/google` verifies an ID token with `googleClient.verifyIdToken` and rejects unverified emails, but if verification fails **or** `idToken` is omitted, it falls back to `GET https://www.googleapis.com/oauth2/v3/userinfo` with a raw Bearer **access token** and hardcodes `emailVerified: true` (687–695). No `aud` binding to `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`. Concrete trigger: any Google OAuth access token (from an unrelated Google Cloud client) that can call userinfo mints a KC JWT. Third unauthenticated login path besides resolve-id and Firebase JWKS. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/controllers/auth.controller.ts` (613–698, especially 660–695) -> Action: Require a verified ID token with explicit `audience`; never accept access-token userinfo as a login proof; do not force `emailVerified: true`.

 * **Profile mass-assignment + password IDOR on `PUT /api/users/:id`.** `UpdateUserBody` accepts `password`, `roles`, `firebase_uid`, `parent_manager_id`, `hierarchy_level`. Controller uses `JwtAuthGuard` only — **no ownership check and no `req.user`**. Lookup is `id::text = $1 OR LOWER(email) = LOWER($1) OR firebase_uid = $1 OR google_id = $1` (267). Root-admin block (281–297) covers **only** `roles` / `parent_manager_id` / `hierarchy_level`; `password` is hashed and written (303–307) and `firebase_uid` is written (345–347) even for the root-admin row. `parent_manager_id`/`hierarchy_level` are on the DTO and blocked for root-admin but **not written** to SQL for other users. Concrete trigger: any JWT (including one minted by resolve-id) `PUT /api/users/<victim-uuid-or-email>` with `{ "password": "...", "roles": ["admin"], "firebase_uid": "<attacker>" }` resets the victim’s password, escalates roles (except the hardcoded root-admin email’s roles), and hijacks Firebase identity. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (126–133); `apps/api/src/services/user-profile.service.ts` (`UpdateUserBody` 18–32, lookup 267, root-admin block 281–297, password 303–307, firebase_uid/roles 345–361) -> Action: Bind updates to `req.user.userId`; reject `roles`/`firebase_uid` except via admin-only endpoints; require current password (or admin) to change password; do not resolve PUT targets by email/firebase_uid/google_id.

 * **Unauthenticated user directory and PII.** `GET /api/users` and `GET /api/users/:id` have no guards. `getUserById` SELECTs `email` and `phone` and matches UUID **or** email / `firebase_uid` / `google_id` (126–157). `getUsers` SELECTs `u.email` (515) plus roles, manager email, and hierarchy. Concrete trigger: `GET /api/users` dumps the directory; `GET /api/users/<email>` returns that user’s phone. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (121–123, 135–149); `apps/api/src/services/user-profile.service.ts` (getUserById ~126–157, getUsers ~515) -> Action: Require auth; redact phone/email/roles for non-self, non-admin callers; stop identifier-OR lookup on a public GET.

 * **Client-supplied hierarchy actors skip authorization when omitted or not found.** `setManagerEnsureRequesterAdminIfProvided` returns `null` (allow) when `requestingUserId` is omitted **or** the user row is not found (492–517). Promote and `manageHierarchy` trust body admin/manager IDs with no JWT binding. `GET eligible-for-promotion/:adminId`, `GET hierarchy/tree`, and `GET :id/hierarchy` have **no** `UseGuards`. Concrete trigger: omit `requestingAdminId` / `requestingUserId` and mutate hierarchy; or scrape the full tree unauthenticated. - File: `apps/api/src/services/user-hierarchy.service.ts` (492–517, 871–914, 945–980); `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (91–108) -> Action: Require `JwtAuthGuard` + `AdminAuthGuard`; bind actor exclusively from `req.user.userId`; fail closed if the actor is missing.

 * **Spoofable follow graph.** `POST/DELETE /api/users/:id/follow` are JWT-gated but `UserFollowService` inserts/deletes using body `follower_id`, not `req.user.userId`. Concrete trigger: authenticated user A follows/unfollows as user B. - File: `apps/api/src/services/user-follow.service.ts` (body `follower_id`); `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (165–180) -> Action: Bind `follower_id` exclusively from the JWT.

 * **Unauthenticated chat writes impersonate `sender_id`.** Class-level `OptionalAuthGuard` always returns `true` (338). Unauthenticated `POST /api/chat/messages` uses body `sender_id` (456–464). Authenticated callers are bound to JWT (450–455) — guests are not. Mark-read falls back to `body.user_id`. Concrete trigger: `POST /api/chat/messages` with `{ "sender_id": "<victim>", "content": "..." }` (and a conversation id / participants) inserts a message attributed to the victim. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/guards/jwt-auth.guard.ts` (308–339); `apps/api/src/controllers/chat.controller.ts` (46, 441–465, 679, 759) -> Action: Require `JwtAuthGuard` on write (and preferably read) paths; never accept client `sender_id`.

 * **Unguarded mutating APIs (rides, items, CRM, community-members, collections, dedicated-items, rate-limit).** No `UseGuards` on `RidesController` (book spoofs `passenger_id` 374–468), `ItemsDeliveryController` (create with caller `owner_id`), `DedicatedItemsController`, `CrmController`, `CommunityMembersController`, `ItemsController` collections CRUD (`POST/PUT/DELETE` 80–108), and `RateLimitController` (`DELETE /rate-limit/clear` 155–167 deletes both window and block keys). Redis-test (get/set/delete/keys) is registered **only when `NODE_ENV !== production`** (`app.module.ts` 122) — Railway `railway.toml.dev` uses `NODE_ENV=development`, so it is live on that service. Concrete trigger: unauthenticated `POST /api/rides/:id/book` with another user’s `passenger_id`; `DELETE /rate-limit/clear` with `{ "identifier": "eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1", "ruleType": "api_access" }` lifts the shared JWT rate-limit bucket. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/rides.controller.ts`; `items-delivery.controller.ts`; `apps/api/src/items/dedicated-items.controller.ts`; `crm.controller.ts`; `community-members.controller.ts`; `apps/api/src/items/items.controller.ts`; `rate-limit.controller.ts`; `redis-test.controller.ts`; `app.module.ts` (122) -> Action: `JwtAuthGuard` (admin where appropriate) on all mutating routes; bind actor IDs from JWT; remove redis-test and rate-limit clear from deployed environments.

 * **JwtAuthGuard rate-limit key is the shared HS256 header prefix.** `checkRateLimit(token.substring(0, 16), "api_access")` (96–114). Issued header is always `{alg:HS256,typ:JWT}`, so the first 16 chars are always `eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1`. 100 req/60s then 5-minute block is **global for all JWT users**. Thrown as `UnauthorizedException` (401). Mobile `authFetchInterceptor` treats 401 as logout (75–89). Combined with unauthenticated `DELETE /rate-limit/clear`, an attacker can both trip a platform-wide 401-logout and clear the block. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/guards/jwt-auth.guard.ts` (96–114); `apps/api/src/auth/services/jwt.service.ts` (276–281); `apps/api/src/auth/services/rate-limit.service.ts` (82–141, 181–192); `apps/mobile/auth/interceptors/authFetchInterceptor.ts` (75–89) -> Action: Rate-limit by `userId` (after verify) or a hash of the full token; return 429 not 401; do not expose unauthenticated clear.

 * **Admin-files list ACL is JWT-only.** `GET /api/admin-files` and `GET folders` use `JwtAuthGuard`; POST/DELETE correctly use `AdminAuthGuard`. Concrete trigger: any authenticated user lists Finance/HR file URLs. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/admin-files.controller.ts` (80–140 vs 142–203) -> Action: Use `AdminAuthGuard` on GET/folders as well.

 * **Unauthenticated donation analytics and real-time presence.** `GET /api/stats/analytics/categories` returns donation money SUM with no guard (439–496). `GET /api/stats/real-time` returns `users_online` and last-hour `user_activities` (782–831). `analytics/users` correctly uses `AdminAuthGuard`. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/stats.controller.ts` (439–496, 782–831) -> Action: Auth-gate (admin for money totals); do not expose presence without auth.

 * **Demo session controller: unauthenticated session enumeration and forced logout.** Demo `SessionController` (`/session`) has no guards. `POST /session/login` verifies real `password_hash` via argon2. `GET /session/user/:userId` lists sessions; `DELETE /session/logout/:sessionId` and `DELETE /session/logout-all/:userId` destroy sessions without proving possession. Concrete trigger: `DELETE /session/logout-all/<victim-uuid>` signs the victim out of all devices. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/controllers/session.controller.ts` (27–229) -> Action: Remove from production/dev deployments; if kept for tests, bind to `JwtAuthGuard` + ownership and disable login.

 * **Unauthenticated Google Places proxy and unencoded `place_id`.** `GET /autocomplete`, `/place-details`, `/search-stats` have no guards and spend `GOOGLE_API_KEY`. `/place-details` interpolates `place_id` without `encodeURIComponent` (line 75), so `&`-bearing values append extra query parameters to Google’s API. `/search-stats` enumerates Redis `search_activity:*` / `places:autocomplete:*` keys. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/places.controller.ts` (9–103) -> Action: Auth + throttle; encode `place_id`; hide search-stats behind admin.

 * **Request-path and startup `DROP TABLE ... CASCADE`.** `PostsController.migrateExistingPostsTableColumns` / `ensurePostsTable` can `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS posts CASCADE` on a live request if `id`/`author_id` are missing (line 78). Related drops: `post_likes` (309), `comment_likes` + `post_comments` (373–374). `tasks-schema.service.ts:41` drops `posts` via task side-effects. `DatabaseInit` drops legacy JSONB `donations` (568) and `rides` (670) on `onModuleInit` when a `data` column is detected. Concrete trigger: a schema-compat mismatch (or a poisoned `information_schema` view in a compromised DB role) destroys posts/likes/comments and their FKs during ordinary traffic or process boot. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/posts.controller.ts` (78, 309, 373–374); `apps/api/src/tasks/tasks-schema.service.ts` (41); `apps/api/src/database/database.init.ts` (568, 670) -> Action: Never DROP on the request path; replace with expand-only migrations; gate startup drops behind an explicit one-shot env flag.

 * **Committed credentials and PII backups in git.** `apps/api/railway.toml.dev` hardcodes a development `JWT_SECRET` (line 34). `docs/SSOT/runbooks/api/environment-separation.md` embeds a production `JWT_SECRET` (line 126; line 102 is a placeholder). Values are not repeated here. `apps/api/src/main.ts` (149–218) fingerprints environments by matching **database password and Redis password/host substrings** committed in source — rotate those credentials and replace the check with an explicit `ENVIRONMENT` flag. 49 tracked JSON files under `apps/api/data-backups/20251224-162421/` include `user_profiles.json` whose keys are `email`, `phone`, and `password_hash`. The env cross-check does not prevent these files from shipping. - File: `apps/api/railway.toml.dev:34`; `docs/SSOT/runbooks/api/environment-separation.md:126`; `apps/api/src/main.ts` (149–218); `apps/api/data-backups/20251224-162421/*` -> Action: Rotate all exposed JWT secrets and DB/Redis credentials whose fragments appear in git; purge secrets from git history; gitignore backups; use secret managers only.

 * **JWT-gated but unscoped task list.** `GET /api/tasks` uses `JwtAuthGuard` then lists by query filters (`assignee`, `status`, …) with **no** restriction to the caller’s tasks. Any authenticated user can enumerate all tasks (ClosedRoute even queries another profile’s `assignee`). - File: `apps/api/src/tasks/tasks.controller.ts` (74–146) -> Action: Default-scope to `req.user`; require admin for global lists.

 * **Unthrottled primary mobile login/register.** SRS §2.1.2 and §3.1 require `ThrottlerGuard` on auth. `AuthController` (`/auth/*`) has class-level `ThrottlerGuard`, but the **primary mobile path** (`apiService.ts` / `dbConfig.USER_LOGIN`) calls `POST /api/users/login` and `POST /api/users/register` on `UsersController`, which have **no** throttle and no global `APP_GUARD`. Concrete trigger: unbounded credential stuffing against `/api/users/login` (and account-creation spam on `/api/users/register`) bypasses the documented 60 req/60s auth limit. Magnified by the password-skip finding: stuffing is unnecessary if email alone succeeds. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (111–118); `apps/mobile/utils/apiService.ts` (463–467); `apps/mobile/utils/dbConfig.ts`; `apps/api/src/app.module.ts` (72–77) -> Action: Apply `ThrottlerGuard` (stricter login tier) to `/api/users/login` and `/register`; register global `APP_GUARD`; keep `/auth/*` and `/api/users/*` auth surfaces consistent.

 * **JWT refresh persists stale roles.** SRS §2.1.4 requires refresh to **fetch latest roles from the database** so demotions propagate. `JwtService.refreshAccessToken` copies `payload.roles` from the presented refresh token and issues a new access token with those roles; there is no `user_profiles` lookup. Redis existence of the refresh token **is** checked inside `verifyToken` (123–129). HMAC compare uses `!==` rather than `timingSafeEqual` (308). Concrete trigger: an admin is demoted to `user` while still holding a valid 30-day refresh token; `POST /auth/refresh` continues to mint access tokens with `roles: ["admin"]` until that refresh token expires or is revoked in Redis. Complements profile mass-assignment (attacker can also *gain* roles) by letting a formerly privileged user *keep* them after a legitimate demotion. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/services/jwt.service.ts` (146–177, especially 157–165, compare at 308); `apps/api/src/auth/controllers/auth.controller.ts` (981–998) -> Action: On refresh, load `roles` from `user_profiles` by `payload.userId`; reject refresh if the user is inactive; use `crypto.timingSafeEqual`; consider rotating the refresh token.

---

## 🐞 Bugs & Logic Issues

 * **Ride booking approval can oversell seats.** `updateBookingStatus` sets status to `approved` then only marks the ride `full` when remaining ≤ 0; no capacity reject and no `FOR UPDATE` row lock before approve. Concurrent approvals can exceed `available_seats`. Booking create does check remaining seats (407–410) but that check is not applied on approve. - Recommendation: In one transaction, lock the ride + sum approved seats; reject approve when `seats_requested` would exceed remaining capacity. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/rides.controller.ts` (505–555).

 * **Unauthenticated real-time stats cartesian join.** `GET /api/stats/real-time` has no guard (already critical-adjacent for `users_online` + last-hour activity). The donations/rides subquery is `FROM donations d FULL OUTER JOIN rides r ON 1=1`, a full cartesian product. Concrete trigger: with D donation rows and R ride rows, Postgres materializes D×R rows before `COUNT(CASE WHEN d.status = 'active')`, so `active_donations` is inflated by ~R and `active_rides` by ~D. As tables grow this is also an unauthenticated CPU/memory DoS. - Recommendation: Replace with two independent `COUNT(*)` queries (or `UNION ALL` of aggregates); auth-gate the endpoint. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/stats.controller.ts` (782–831, join at 803–809).

 * **Task done PATCH can create duplicate completion posts.** `evaluateMarkDoneTimeLogRequirement` skips when `oldStatus === "done"`, but `runUpdateTaskCompletionPosts` still runs whenever `body.status === "done"` (`tasks-patch-mutation.service.ts:380–381`) with no transition check. `finalizePerformerCompletionPost` INSERTs a new `task_completion` row when no matching `task_assignment` title is updated, so a second PATCH to `done` duplicates the feed post. - Recommendation: Gate completion side effects on `oldStatus !== "done"`; add an idempotent unique constraint / upsert for completion posts.

 * **Auth refresh waiters hang on failure.** `apps/mobile/auth/interceptors/authFetchInterceptor.ts` (75–89): failed refresh clears `refreshSubscribers` without resolving waiters subscribed via `subscribeTokenRefresh`. Parallel 401s wait forever. Combined with the JwtAuthGuard 401-on-quota finding, a shared rate-limit 401 can both log out the initiating client and hang sibling in-flight requests. - Recommendation: Reject/resolve all subscribers on failure paths before clearing tokens; do not treat 429/quota as auth failure.

 * **ClosedRoute task type inconsistency.** `ClosedRoute.tsx` filters existing content with `type === 'task'` (~324) but pushes `type: 'task_post'` (~351), so duplicate suppression against post-derived tasks is ineffective. - Recommendation: Align filter and push types (or normalize via a single type constant matching `FeedItem.type`).

 * **Spoofable post comment author.** `POST /api/posts/:postId/comments` is JWT-gated but uses body `user_id`, not `req.user.userId`. Any authenticated user can attribute comments to another account. - Recommendation: Bind `user_id` exclusively from the JWT. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/posts.controller.ts` (1573–1584).

 * **Unauthenticated knowledge-link writes.** `POST /api/donations/knowledge/links` is explicitly public and inserts caller-supplied URL/display name (optional spoofed `createdByUserId`). Spam/defacement surface. DELETE is JWT-gated and **does** check `admin` / `super_admin` / `org_admin` in-handler (312–318) — decorator is JWT-only. - Recommendation: Require auth; rate-limit; ignore client `createdByUserId`. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/donations.controller.ts` (226–291, 304–318).

 * **`PUT /api/posts/:postId` owner check falls back to body `user_id`.** Guard sets `req.user.userId` (JWT payload), so the fallback is currently dead in the happy path; if the payload shape ever omitted `userId`/`id`/`sub`, a caller could pass another user’s id and pass the `author_id` check. Same fallback exists on related post mutate handlers (~2445, ~2531). - Recommendation: Remove `body.user_id` fallback; compare `req.user.userId` only. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/posts.controller.ts` (2283–2332). (The 2026-05-04 `images` TEXT[] mapping in commit `073722f` is otherwise correct.)

 * **Posts / tasks / dedicated-items catch blocks leak `error.message` to clients.** Contrasts CODE_QUALITY `constraints.md` (never send stack/driver text to clients). Firebase verify also returns `error.message` (`jwt-auth.guard.ts` 211–213). - Recommendation: Map to stable error codes; log details server-side only.

 * **Register/login write Hebrew UI strings into stored profile defaults** (`bio`, `city`) in `user-auth.service.ts` (120–126, 500, 505). Violates English-only code/data defaults; i18n should live in the client. - Recommendation: Store locale-neutral defaults; let the client localize empty states.

## 🏗️ Architecture & Clean Code

 * **No Domain / Application / Infrastructure folders exist** under `apps/` or `packages/` (count 0). Target in `docs/SSOT/CODE_QUALITY/layers-and-boundaries.md` is Infrastructure → Application → Domain (inward). Closest pure islands: `apps/api/src/tasks/task-completion-participants.ts`, `tasks-validation.ts`, `tasks-query-params.ts`, `tasks.types.ts` (no Nest/pg imports). `packages/` still only ships `config-eslint`.

 * **Fat Nest controllers own SQL + HTTP + DDL.** Worst offenders (LOC): `posts.controller.ts` (2741) — Pool + Redis + `DROP TABLE`; `stats.controller.ts` (1709); `community-group-challenges.controller.ts` (1097); `donations.controller.ts` (892); `chat.controller.ts` (813); `rides.controller.ts` (779). `items-delivery.service.ts` is misplaced under `controllers/`. SRS §5.2 already notes donations/rides logic in controllers.

 * **Better-shaped but still not Clean Architecture:** `users.controller.ts` is thin and delegates to services — those services still inject `pg.Pool`. `items/` controller → `ItemsService`. `tasks/` is split into services; the controller still injects Pool for some paths. Positive contrasts: `CommunityGroupChallengesController` binds creator from JWT; `ChallengesController` scopes to `CurrentUser`; `NotificationsController.validateOwnership` binds URL `userId` to `req.user`.

 * **Operator matching module still missing** (SRS §2.14 / §5.1 / §10.1): no `/api/operator/*`, no matching tables, no mobile Shiduchim Tov screens. `OperatorAuthGuard` exists in `jwt-auth.guard.ts` but has no consumers. Anonymity column still absent from posts DDL.

 * **Mobile debt hubs:** `utils/databaseService.ts` (1225), `apiService.ts` (1055), `enhancedDatabaseService.ts` (897); duplicate `google_auth/SecureApiService.ts` (1165). Duplicate mappers `mapPostToFeedItemForItemsScreen` / `ForTrumpScreen` vs `useFeedData` (logged as follow-ups in `tech-debt-log.md`). Largest tracked source: `apps/mobile/utils/bubbles_Data.tsx` (4852). **197** source `.ts`/`.tsx` files >200 LOC (apps+packages, excluding node_modules/dist/spec/test).

 * **ThrottlerModule is configured but not registered as `APP_GUARD`** (`app.module.ts` 72–77). Only `AuthController` and `NotificationsController` opt in. Global 60 req/60s (SRS §3.1) is therefore not actually global.

 * **Custom HMAC JWT** (SRS §10.3 Medium): hand-rolled in `jwt.service.ts` rather than `jose`/`jsonwebtoken`. Functional, but `alg` is never pinned on verify (signature is always HMAC over the provided header, so `alg=none` does not skip verify — defense-in-depth still missing); compare is not constant-time.

## 🚨 Critical Vulnerabilities

 * **Unauthenticated identifier-only account takeover via `POST /api/users/resolve-id`.** No `JwtAuthGuard`. Supplying a known `email`, `google_id`, or `firebase_uid` looks up `user_profiles`, optionally **links** a caller-supplied `firebase_uid`/`google_id` onto that row, then **mints a full JWT pair** (`accessToken` + `refreshToken`) for that user. If Admin SDK is initialized and no local row matches, `tryAutoCreateUserFromFirebase` calls `admin.auth().getUser(firebase_uid)` **without an ID token** and provisions a KC user + tokens from that UID. Concrete trigger: `POST /api/users/resolve-id` with `{ "email": "<victim>" }` returns tokens with the victim’s `roles`. SRS §2.1.6 currently documents this minting behavior — the spec itself should be tightened to require proof of possession (verified Firebase/Google ID token or existing session), not identifier matching. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (183–188) → `apps/api/src/services/user-auth.service.ts` (`resolveUserId` 270–342, `tryAutoCreateUserFromFirebase` 450–575, `linkResolveIdentifiers` 580–627, `buildResolveSuccessResponse` 645–672) -> Action: Remove token minting from resolve-id; require a verified ID token; never link identifiers from an unauthenticated body; bind any remaining resolve helper to `JwtAuthGuard`.

 * **Primary mobile login accepts email-only (password verification skipped).** `POST /api/users/login` (`UsersController` / `UserAuthService.loginUser`) verifies argon2 **only when both** `loginData.password` and `user.password_hash` are truthy (223–231). Omitting `password`, or targeting a row with a null hash, falls through to `success: true` and returns email, phone, roles, `parent_manager_id`, and other profile fields. `/auth/login` correctly rejects missing hash and invalid passwords (528–541). Concrete trigger: `POST /api/users/login` with `{ "email": "<victim>" }` (no password). Combined with resolve-id, the documented mobile sequence (`apiService.loginUser` then `resolveUserId`) is an email-only takeover. Contrasts SRS §2.1.2 (`argon2.verify`). - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (116–118); `apps/api/src/services/user-auth.service.ts` (190–263, especially 223–231); contrast `apps/api/src/auth/controllers/auth.controller.ts` (501–541); mobile `apps/mobile/utils/apiService.ts` (463–467) / `dbConfig.ts` (`USER_LOGIN`) -> Action: Always require a password; fail closed if hash is missing; never return `success: true` without `argon2.verify`; stop treating resolve-id as a post-login token mint.

 * **Unauthenticated root-admin bootstrap via hardcoded email (new 2026-08-24).** `KC_ROOT_ADMIN_EMAIL_FALLBACK` is committed (`karmacommunity2.0@gmail.com`). Unauthenticated `POST /api/users/register` with that email sets `initialRoles = ["user", "admin"]` (101–102) when the row does not yet exist. Unauthenticated `POST /api/users/login` with the same email runs `array_append(roles, 'admin')` **before** the optional password check (212–220), then succeeds without a password (223–231). Concrete trigger A (empty DB / missing row): `POST /api/users/register` with that email (password optional) creates an admin. Concrete trigger B (row exists): `POST /api/users/login` with `{ "email": "<root-admin>" }` (no password) writes `admin` if missing, returns PII, then `POST /api/users/resolve-id` mints an admin JWT. SRS §2.1.1 allows first-login promotion only as a bootstrap — not as an unauthenticated, passwordless grant. - File: `apps/api/src/config/kc-root-admin.defaults.ts`; `apps/api/src/services/user-auth.service.ts` (101–102, 212–231); `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (111–118, 183–188) -> Action: Require a verified secret/env match plus password (or disable public register for that email); never mutate roles before `argon2.verify`; bind bootstrap to a one-shot setup command, not a public API.

 * **Unauthenticated register binds attacker-chosen Firebase UID.** `POST /api/users/register` has no guard. `RegisterUserBody` accepts `firebase_uid` and `id`; the INSERT writes `userData.firebase_uid || userData.id || null` into `user_profiles.firebase_uid` (139). Password is optional (97–99). Concrete trigger: `POST /api/users/register` with `{ "email": "attacker@example.com", "firebase_uid": "<victim Firebase UID>" }` (or `{ "id": "<victim Firebase UID>" }`). The victim’s later Firebase ID-token path (`JwtAuthGuard` lookup by `firebase_uid`) attaches the attacker’s KC row. Complements resolve-id linking and profile `firebase_uid` mass-assignment as a **fourth** unauthenticated identity-binding path. - File: `apps/api/src/services/user-auth.service.ts` (`RegisterUserBody` 20–33, hash optional 97–99, bind at 139); `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (111–113) -> Action: Ignore client `firebase_uid`/`id` on register; require password; bind Firebase UID only after verified ID-token proof.

 * **Firebase JWKS fallback accepts any Firebase project.** When the Admin SDK is not initialized, `verifyIdTokenWithPublicJwks` takes `aud`/`iss` from the token and only compares them to `FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID` **if that env var is set**. File header states this is intentional. Concrete trigger: attacker creates their own Firebase project, obtains a valid Google-signed ID token, then (via unauthenticated `resolve-id` linking, register `firebase_uid` bind, or authenticated `PUT /api/users/:id` `firebase_uid` mass-assignment) binds that UID to a victim and presents the ID token to any `JwtAuthGuard` route. Complements resolve-id/mass-assignment with a **second login mechanism** that never mints a server JWT. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/services/firebase-admin.service.ts` (11–14, 150–185, 191–264); `apps/api/src/auth/guards/jwt-auth.guard.ts` (117–134, 170–213) -> Action: Require `FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID` (or service-account `project_id`) and reject tokens whose `aud` does not match; fail closed if unset in deployed environments.

 * **Google OAuth access-token fallback bypasses ID-token audience and email verification.** `POST /auth/google` verifies an ID token with `googleClient.verifyIdToken` and rejects unverified emails, but if verification throws **or no `idToken` is sent**, it falls through to `GET https://www.googleapis.com/oauth2/v3/userinfo` with a raw `accessToken` Bearer and sets `emailVerified: true` unconditionally. Concrete trigger: `POST /auth/google` with `{ "accessToken": "<Google OAuth access token issued to any client>" }` (no `idToken`) mints a KC JWT for that Google email. This skips the `aud` check that prevents tokens from attacker-controlled OAuth clients, contradicting SRS §2.1.3 (“Token verified via google-auth-library”). Complements resolve-id / Firebase JWKS as a **third** unauthenticated login path. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/controllers/auth.controller.ts` (613–698, especially 660–695) -> Action: Accept only ID tokens; pass an explicit audience list of this app’s client IDs; never treat userinfo access tokens as a sign-in proof; honor `email_verified` from Google.

 * **Authenticated privilege escalation via profile mass-assignment, plus password IDOR (including root admin) (new precision 2026-08-24).** `PUT /api/users/:id` is JWT-gated but does **not** check `req.user.userId === :id` and never receives `req.user`. The lookup is `id::text = $1 OR LOWER(email) = LOWER($1) OR firebase_uid = $1 OR google_id = $1` (267), so the path param can be the victim’s email. `UpdateUserBody` accepts `password`, `roles`, `firebase_uid`, `parent_manager_id`, and `hierarchy_level`. **Applied to SQL today:** `password` is argon2-hashed and written (303–307); `firebase_uid` (345–347); `roles` (349–361). The hardcoded root-admin email block (281–297) rejects only `roles` / `parent_manager_id` / `hierarchy_level` — **not** `password` or `firebase_uid`. Concrete trigger A: any JWT (including one minted by resolve-id) `PUT /api/users/<victim-email>` with `{ "roles": ["super_admin"] }` except the hardcoded root-admin email. Concrete trigger B: the same caller sends `{ "password": "<attacker-chosen>" }` against **any** user including the root-admin row, then logs in on `/api/users/login` / `/session/login`. `parent_manager_id` / `hierarchy_level` remain DTO-only (used to block the root-admin row at 281–297; not written). ValidationPipe whitelist cannot strip these fields because `UpdateUserBody` is a TypeScript type, not a class-validator class. - File: `apps/api/src/services/user-profile.service.ts` (18–32, 254–361, lookup 267, root-admin block 281–297, password 303–307); `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (126–133) -> Action: Enforce ownership or `AdminAuthGuard`; never accept client `password`/`roles`/`firebase_uid` on this route; use a dedicated authenticated change-password endpoint bound to `req.user`; extend the root-admin freeze to password and identity fields.

 * **Unauthenticated PII dump of all users.** `GET /api/users` and `GET /api/users/:id` (also `GET /api/users/search`) have no guards. List query selects `u.email`; get-by-id selects `email` and `phone` and matches UUID **or** email / `firebase_uid` / `google_id` (152–157), so `GET /api/users/<email>` is an unauthenticated identity oracle. Also unauthenticated: `GET :id/activities`, `GET :id/stats`, `GET stats/summary`. Violates SRS §3.5 data minimization. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (40–43, 101–163, 121–123, 135–149); `apps/api/src/services/user-profile.service.ts` (126–157, 515) -> Action: Require auth; project fields by viewer; never return email/phone to unauthorized callers; look up by UUID only.

 * **Client-supplied hierarchy actors (privilege escalation).** Hierarchy mutate routes use `JwtAuthGuard` but authorization is based on **body** `requestingAdminId` / `requestingUserId` / `managerId`, not `req.user`. Omitting `requestingUserId` on `setManager` skips the admin check (`setManagerEnsureRequesterAdminIfProvided` returns `null` when the id is omitted **or** when the supplied id is not found). An attacker with any valid JWT can send another admin’s UUID and promote/demote. Unauthenticated reads: `GET eligible-for-promotion/:adminId`, `GET hierarchy/tree`, `GET :id/hierarchy`. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (45–108); `apps/api/src/services/user-hierarchy.service.ts` (492–517, 871–914, 945–980) -> Action: Bind actor exclusively to `req.user`; require `AdminAuthGuard` (or volunteer-manager policy) on mutations; authenticate tree/eligible reads.

 * **Spoofable follow graph.** `POST/DELETE /api/users/:id/follow` is JWT-gated but inserts `followData.follower_id` from the body, not `req.user.userId`. Any authenticated user can create or delete follows as any other user. - File: `apps/api/src/services/user-follow.service.ts` (6–8, 19–34); `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (165–180) -> Action: Use `req.user.userId` as follower; ignore body actor ids.

 * **Chat is effectively public (reads and writes).** Class-level `@UseGuards(OptionalAuthGuard)` **always returns `true`** (`jwt-auth.guard.ts:338`). Guest path for conversations uses the URL `userId` (`chat.controller.ts:267–268`). Guest path for messages skips participant checks (`378–387`) and returns conversation messages. Unauthenticated `POST` chat `/messages` requires `sender_id` and then sends as that user (`441–465`) — concrete trigger: `POST /messages` with a victim UUID impersonates them. Mark-read endpoints fall back to `body.user_id` (`679`, `759`). - File: `apps/api/src/auth/guards/jwt-auth.guard.ts` (308–339); `apps/api/src/controllers/chat.controller.ts` (46, 250–270, 378–387, 441–465, 671–679, 751–759) -> Action: Require `JwtAuthGuard`; always bind sender/reader to `req.user`; never accept body/URL actor ids.

 * **Rides: no auth; spoofable passenger; unauthenticated booking status.** `rides.controller.ts` has **zero** `@UseGuards`. `POST :id/book` uses body `passenger_id` (email / firebase_uid / UUID). `PUT bookings/:bookingId/status` updates status from the body with no driver/admin check. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/rides.controller.ts` (37, 374–468, 505–555) -> Action: Add `JwtAuthGuard`; bind passenger to `req.user`; restrict status changes to driver/admin; reject approve when remaining seats would go negative (see Bugs).

 * **Unauthenticated mutating admin/CRM/community/items surfaces.** No `@UseGuards` on: `CrmController` (`/api/crm`), `CommunityMembersController` (`/api/community-members`, includes `contact_info` email/phone), `ItemsDeliveryController` (`/api/items-delivery`, client `owner_id`), `DedicatedItemsController` (`/api/dedicated-items` create/update/delete), `ItemsController` (`/api/collections/:collection` CRUD allowlist includes `users`, `chats`, `messages`, `tasks`, `notifications`, `rides`, `organizations`). Concrete trigger: unauthenticated `POST /api/crm`, `DELETE /api/community-members/:id`, `POST /api/collections/messages/...`. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/crm.controller.ts`; `community-members.controller.ts`; `items-delivery.controller.ts`; `apps/api/src/items/dedicated-items.controller.ts`; `apps/api/src/items/items.controller.ts` + `items.service.ts` (15–36) -> Action: Auth + role/ownership on all mutating routes; lock down or remove generic collections CRUD.

 * **Admin shared-file metadata is JWT-readable by any user.** `GET /api/admin-files` and `GET /api/admin-files/folders` use `JwtAuthGuard` only. Create/delete correctly require `AdminAuthGuard`. Concrete trigger: any JWT (including one minted by resolve-id) lists `general_files` rows, including `folder_path` values such as Finance/HR and the stored `url`. Combined with the unauthenticated login paths this is a cross-user document leak, not just an admin-console convenience. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/admin-files.controller.ts` (80–140 vs 142–203) -> Action: Apply `AdminAuthGuard` to list/folder reads (or a dedicated file-share ACL); never treat “has a JWT” as admin-file authorization.

 * **Unauthenticated Redis session/token store access (non-production).** `RedisTestController` (`/redis-test`) has no guards. `app.module.ts:122` registers it when `NODE_ENV !== "production"`. `railway.toml.dev` sets `NODE_ENV = "development"`, so the **dev Railway service** exposes `GET /redis-test/keys?pattern=*`, `GET /redis-test/get/:key`, `POST /redis-test/set`, `DELETE /redis-test/delete/:key` against the session/token store. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/redis-test.controller.ts` (16–124); `apps/api/src/app.module.ts` (122) -> Action: Remove from all deployed builds (including development) or gate with `AdminAuthGuard` + local-only bind.

 * **Unauthenticated rate-limit bypass, including the shared JWT bucket.** `DELETE /rate-limit/clear` (and test/stats/rules/custom/simulate) has no auth. `clearRateLimit` deletes both the window key and the block key. Combined with the shared HS256-header identifier, `DELETE /rate-limit/clear` with `{ "identifier": "eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1", "ruleType": "api_access" }` resets the **global** authenticated quota and 5-minute block for every JWT user. Combined with missing global `APP_GUARD`, SRS §3.1 “global 60 req/60s via ThrottlerGuard” is not actually enforced. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/rate-limit.controller.ts` (20–175); `apps/api/src/auth/services/rate-limit.service.ts` (181–192); `apps/api/src/app.module.ts` (72–77) — `ThrottlerModule.forRoot` present, **no `APP_GUARD` / `ThrottlerGuard` registration anywhere in `apps/api/src`** except Auth + Notifications -> Action: Register global `ThrottlerGuard`; delete or admin-gate the debug controller.

 * **JwtAuthGuard authenticated rate-limit is a global shared bucket.** `canActivate` calls `rateLimitService.checkRateLimit(token.substring(0, 16), "api_access", { requests: 100, windowMs: 60000, blockDurationMs: 300000 })`. This server’s access tokens are signed with a **fixed** header `{ alg: "HS256", typ: "JWT" }` whose first 16 characters are always `eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1`. Redis keys `rate_limit:api_access:eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1` and `blocked:api_access:eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1` are shared. Concrete trigger (Redis up): any valid JWT holder sends 100 requests to any `JwtAuthGuard` route within 60s; **all** subsequent JWT-authenticated calls from every user are denied for 5 minutes. Denial is `throw new UnauthorizedException("Rate limit exceeded")` (HTTP 401, not 429). Mobile `authFetchInterceptor` treats every 401 as refresh-then-`clearTokens`/`clearSession` (75–89), so the shared bucket can mass-log-out clients. Fail-open if Redis is down (does not apply then). - File: `apps/api/src/auth/guards/jwt-auth.guard.ts` (96–114); `apps/api/src/auth/services/jwt.service.ts` (276–281); `apps/api/src/auth/services/rate-limit.service.ts` (82–141); `apps/mobile/auth/interceptors/authFetchInterceptor.ts` (75–89) -> Action: Identify by `payload.userId` (after verify) or a hash of the full token; return 429; never 401 for quota; do not clear mobile sessions on 429.

 * **Unauthenticated session enumeration and forced logout.** Demo `SessionController` (`/session`) has no guards. `POST /session/login` verifies real `password_hash` via argon2. `GET /session/user/:userId` lists sessions; `DELETE /session/logout/:sessionId` and `DELETE /session/logout-all/:userId` destroy sessions without proving possession. Concrete trigger: `DELETE /session/logout-all/<victim-uuid>` signs the victim out of all devices. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/controllers/session.controller.ts` (27–229) -> Action: Remove from production/dev deployments; if kept for tests, bind to `JwtAuthGuard` + ownership and disable login.

 * **Unauthenticated Google Places proxy and unencoded `place_id`.** `GET /autocomplete`, `/place-details`, `/search-stats` have no guards and spend `GOOGLE_API_KEY`. `/place-details` interpolates `place_id` without `encodeURIComponent` (line 75), so `&`-bearing values append extra query parameters to Google’s API. `/search-stats` enumerates Redis `search_activity:*` / `places:autocomplete:*` keys. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/places.controller.ts` (9–103) -> Action: Auth + throttle; encode `place_id`; hide search-stats behind admin.

 * **Request-path and startup `DROP TABLE ... CASCADE`.** `PostsController.migrateExistingPostsTableColumns` / `ensurePostsTable` can `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS posts CASCADE` on a live request if `id`/`author_id` are missing (line 78). Related drops: `post_likes` (309), `comment_likes` + `post_comments` (373–374). `tasks-schema.service.ts:41` drops `posts` via task side-effects. `DatabaseInit` drops legacy JSONB `donations` (568) and `rides` (670) on `onModuleInit` when a `data` column is detected. Concrete trigger: a schema-compat mismatch (or a poisoned `information_schema` view in a compromised DB role) destroys posts/likes/comments and their FKs during ordinary traffic or process boot. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/posts.controller.ts` (78, 309, 373–374); `apps/api/src/tasks/tasks-schema.service.ts` (41); `apps/api/src/database/database.init.ts` (568, 670) -> Action: Never DROP on the request path; replace with expand-only migrations; gate startup drops behind an explicit one-shot env flag.

 * **Committed credentials and PII backups in git (expanded 2026-08-24).** `apps/api/railway.toml.dev` hardcodes a development `JWT_SECRET` (line 34). `docs/SSOT/runbooks/api/environment-separation.md` embeds a production `JWT_SECRET` (line 126; line 102 is a placeholder). Values are not repeated here. `apps/api/src/main.ts` (149–218) fingerprints environments by matching **database password and Redis password/host substrings** committed in source — rotate those credentials and replace the check with an explicit `ENVIRONMENT` flag. 49 tracked JSON files under `apps/api/data-backups/20251224-162421/` include `user_profiles.json` whose keys are `email`, `phone`, and `password_hash`. The env cross-check does not prevent these files from shipping. - File: `apps/api/railway.toml.dev:34`; `docs/SSOT/runbooks/api/environment-separation.md:126`; `apps/api/src/main.ts` (149–218); `apps/api/data-backups/20251224-162421/*` -> Action: Rotate all exposed JWT secrets and DB/Redis credentials whose fragments appear in git; purge secrets from git history; gitignore backups; use secret managers only.

 * **JWT-gated but unscoped task list.** `GET /api/tasks` uses `JwtAuthGuard` then lists by query filters (`assignee`, `status`, …) with **no** restriction to the caller’s tasks. Any authenticated user can enumerate all tasks (ClosedRoute even queries another profile’s `assignee`). - File: `apps/api/src/tasks/tasks.controller.ts` (74–146) -> Action: Default-scope to `req.user`; require admin for global lists.

 * **Unthrottled primary mobile login/register.** SRS §2.1.2 and §3.1 require `ThrottlerGuard` on auth. `AuthController` (`/auth/*`) has class-level `ThrottlerGuard`, but the **primary mobile path** (`apiService.ts` / `dbConfig.USER_LOGIN`) calls `POST /api/users/login` and `POST /api/users/register` on `UsersController`, which have **no** throttle and no global `APP_GUARD`. Concrete trigger: unbounded credential stuffing against `/api/users/login` (and account-creation spam on `/api/users/register`) bypasses the documented 60 req/60s auth limit. Magnified by the password-skip finding: stuffing is unnecessary if email alone succeeds. - File: `apps/api/src/controllers/users.controller.ts` (111–118); `apps/mobile/utils/apiService.ts` (463–467); `apps/mobile/utils/dbConfig.ts` (44); `apps/api/src/app.module.ts` (72–77) -> Action: Apply `ThrottlerGuard` (stricter login tier) to `/api/users/login` and `/register`; register global `APP_GUARD`; keep `/auth/*` and `/api/users/*` auth surfaces consistent.

 * **JWT refresh persists stale roles.** SRS §2.1.4 requires refresh to **fetch latest roles from the database** so demotions propagate. `JwtService.refreshAccessToken` copies `payload.roles` from the presented refresh token and issues a new access token with those roles; there is no `user_profiles` lookup. Redis existence of the refresh token **is** checked inside `verifyToken` (123–129). Concrete trigger: an admin is demoted to `user` while still holding a valid 30-day refresh token; `POST /auth/refresh` continues to mint access tokens with `roles: ["admin"]` until that refresh token expires or is revoked in Redis. Complements profile mass-assignment (attacker can also *gain* roles) by letting a formerly privileged user *keep* them after a legitimate demotion. - File: `apps/api/src/auth/services/jwt.service.ts` (146–177, especially 157–165); `apps/api/src/auth/controllers/auth.controller.ts` (981–998) -> Action: On refresh, load `roles` from `user_profiles` by `payload.userId`; reject refresh if the user is inactive; consider rotating the refresh token.

---

## 🐞 Bugs & Logic Issues

 * **Ride booking approval can oversell seats.** `updateBookingStatus` sets status to `approved` then only marks the ride `full` when remaining ≤ 0; no capacity reject and no `FOR UPDATE` row lock before approve. Concurrent approvals can exceed `available_seats`. Booking create does check remaining seats (407–410) but that check is not applied on approve. - Recommendation: In one transaction, lock the ride + sum approved seats; reject approve when `seats_requested` would exceed remaining capacity. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/rides.controller.ts` (505–555).

 * **Unauthenticated real-time stats cartesian join.** `GET /api/stats/real-time` has no guard (already critical-adjacent for `users_online` + last-hour activity). The donations/rides subquery is `FROM donations d FULL OUTER JOIN rides r ON 1=1`, a full cartesian product. Concrete trigger: with D donation rows and R ride rows, Postgres materializes D×R rows before `COUNT(CASE WHEN d.status = 'active')`, so `active_donations` is inflated by ~R and `active_rides` by ~D. As tables grow this is also an unauthenticated CPU/memory DoS. - Recommendation: Replace with two independent `COUNT(*)` queries (or `UNION ALL` of aggregates); auth-gate the endpoint. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/stats.controller.ts` (782–831, join at 803–809).

 * **Task done PATCH can create duplicate completion posts.** `evaluateMarkDoneTimeLogRequirement` skips when `oldStatus === "done"`, but `runUpdateTaskCompletionPosts` still runs whenever `body.status === "done"` (`tasks-patch-mutation.service.ts:380–381`) with no transition check. `finalizePerformerCompletionPost` INSERTs a new `task_completion` row when no matching `task_assignment` title is updated, so a second PATCH to `done` duplicates the feed post. - Recommendation: Gate completion side effects on `oldStatus !== "done"`; add an idempotent unique constraint / upsert for completion posts.

 * **Auth refresh waiters hang on failure.** `apps/mobile/auth/interceptors/authFetchInterceptor.ts` (75–89): failed refresh clears `refreshSubscribers` without resolving waiters subscribed via `subscribeTokenRefresh`. Parallel 401s wait forever. Combined with the JwtAuthGuard 401-on-quota finding, a shared rate-limit 401 can both log out the initiating client and hang sibling in-flight requests. - Recommendation: Reject/resolve all subscribers on failure paths before clearing tokens; do not treat 429/quota as auth failure.

 * **ClosedRoute task type inconsistency.** `ClosedRoute.tsx` filters existing content with `type === 'task'` (~324) but pushes `type: 'task_post'` (~351), so duplicate suppression against post-derived tasks is ineffective. - Recommendation: Align filter and push types (or normalize via a single type constant matching `FeedItem.type`).

 * **Spoofable post comment author.** `POST /api/posts/:postId/comments` is JWT-gated but uses body `user_id`, not `req.user.userId`. Any authenticated user can attribute comments to another account. - Recommendation: Bind `user_id` exclusively from the JWT. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/posts.controller.ts` (1573–1584).

 * **Unauthenticated knowledge-link writes.** `POST /api/donations/knowledge/links` is explicitly public and inserts caller-supplied URL/display name (optional spoofed `createdByUserId`). Spam/defacement surface. DELETE is JWT-gated and **does** check `admin` / `super_admin` / `org_admin` in-handler (312–318) — decorator is JWT-only. - Recommendation: Require auth; rate-limit; ignore client `createdByUserId`. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/donations.controller.ts` (226–291, 304–318).

 * **`PUT /api/posts/:postId` owner check falls back to body `user_id`.** Guard sets `req.user.userId` (JWT payload), so the fallback is currently dead in the happy path; if the payload shape ever omitted `userId`/`id`/`sub`, a caller could pass another user’s id and pass the `author_id` check. Same fallback exists on related post mutate handlers (~2445, ~2531). - Recommendation: Remove `body.user_id` fallback; compare `req.user.userId` only. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/posts.controller.ts` (2283–2332). (The 2026-05-04 `images` TEXT[] mapping in commit `073722f` is otherwise correct.)

 * **Unauthenticated stats analytics / real-time.** `GET /api/stats/analytics/categories` has no guard and returns `SUM` of money donations plus weekly/monthly counts. `GET /api/stats/real-time` has no guard and returns last-hour `user_activities` counts plus `users_online` (profiles with `last_active` in 5 minutes). `GET /api/stats/analytics/users` is correctly `AdminAuthGuard`. Concrete trigger: unauthenticated scrape of donation-money totals and live activity. - Recommendation: Auth or admin-gate money/activity analytics; keep only coarse public community counters unauthenticated. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/stats.controller.ts` (439–496, 782–831).

 * **`SYNC_API_KEY` defaults to `change-me-in-production`.** Sync mutate routes also use `AdminAuthGuard`, so this is not a standalone bypass today, but a missing env var leaves a known default and the extra key check is skipped when the default is still in place (`sync.controller.ts` 422, 511). - Recommendation: Fail closed if unset in production. File: `apps/api/src/controllers/sync.controller.ts` (27–40, 422–424, 511–513).

 * **Client-facing SQL/internal error strings.** Several catch blocks return ``Failed to …: ${errorMessage}`` or raw `error.message` to the HTTP client: `posts.controller` (get-user-posts 1168–1182; add-comment ~1704–1716), `tasks.controller` (list/get/create/update/delete), `dedicated-items.controller`, `items.controller`, and `JwtAuthGuard.verifyFirebaseToken` (`Invalid Firebase token: ${error.message}` at 211–213). Stack traces stay in logs (good), but `error.message` can include Postgres/JWKS detail. Violates `docs/SSOT/CODE_QUALITY/constraints.md` (no technical leaks to clients). - Recommendation: Return a generic client error; log `error.message` server-side only.

 * **Custom JWT HMAC compare is not constant-time.** `verifyTokenSignature` uses `signature !== expectedSignature` (`jwt.service.ts:308`) instead of `crypto.timingSafeEqual`. Combined with a committed `JWT_SECRET` this is not the primary break, but it is a real verification defect on the only signature check. - Recommendation: Decode both sides to buffers of equal length and compare with `timingSafeEqual`; reject mismatched lengths first.

 * **Email enumeration on the primary login path.** `POST /api/users/login` returns `"User not found"` vs `"Invalid password"` (206–229). `/auth/login` correctly uses a generic `"Invalid email or password"`. Combined with password-skip, a missing password on a known email is already a positive existence oracle. - Recommendation: Use a single generic failure for both missing user and bad password; still require `argon2.verify`.

---

## 🏗️ Architecture & Clean Code

 * **Clean Architecture layers are still absent.** Target (`docs/SSOT/CODE_QUALITY/layers-and-boundaries.md`) is Infrastructure → Application → Domain. There are **zero** `/domain`, `/application`, or `/infrastructure` folders under `apps/` or `packages/`. Nest feature folders own HTTP + SQL + Redis in the same class. Closest pure islands remain `apps/api/src/tasks/task-completion-participants.ts`, `tasks-validation.ts`, `tasks-query-params.ts`, `tasks.types.ts` (no Nest/`pg` imports). `packages/` still only ships `config-eslint`.

 * **Fat controllers continue to own DDL and business rules.** Worst API files (unchanged LOC): `posts.controller.ts` (2741, Pool + Redis + `DROP TABLE`), `stats.controller.ts` (1709), `community-group-challenges.controller.ts` (1097), `donations.controller.ts` (892), `chat.controller.ts` (813), `rides.controller.ts` (779). `ItemsDeliveryService` is still misplaced under `controllers/`. Positive contrasts (not CA, but better): `UsersController` is thin over services (services still inject `pg` Pool); `ChallengesController` scopes to `CurrentUser`; `NotificationsController.validateOwnership` binds URL `userId` to `req.user`; community-group-challenges binds creator from JWT.

 * **SRS / code mismatches that remain open.** §2.1.4 (refresh must reload DB roles) is unimplemented. §2.1.2 / §3.1 (`ThrottlerGuard` on login; `argon2.verify`; global 60/60) apply only to `/auth/*` + notifications, not `/api/users/login` (which also skips the password check and auto-promotes the hardcoded root-admin email). §2.1.3 requires Google ID-token verification; access-token userinfo fallback contradicts it. §3.2 lists `OptionalAuthGuard` as “enrich unauthenticated requests”; implementation always allows and chat writes as `sender_id`. §3.5 data minimization is violated by unauthenticated user/email/phone and admin-file list. §10.1 operator matching module / `/api/operator/*` / matching tables still missing (`OperatorAuthGuard` exists unused).

 * **Mobile architecture debt hubs (unchanged).** `utils/databaseService.ts` (1225), `apiService.ts` (1055), `enhancedDatabaseService.ts` (897), duplicate `google_auth/SecureApiService.ts` (1165). Largest tracked source: `apps/mobile/utils/bubbles_Data.tsx` (4852). Duplicate mappers `mapPostToFeedItemForItemsScreen` / `ForTrumpScreen` vs `useFeedData` remain logged as follow-ups in `tech-debt-log.md`.

---

## 🧹 Technical Debt & Maintenance

 * **197 source `.ts`/`.tsx` files exceed the ~200 LOC cap** (apps + packages, excluding `node_modules` / `dist` / spec / test). Same product tree as 2026-08-23 (count 196); difference is find methodology, not new source.

 * **`ThrottlerModule.forRoot` without `APP_GUARD`** (`app.module.ts` 72–77) plus a public `RateLimitController` that can clear the shared JWT bucket. Register the guard globally or stop claiming SRS §3.1 compliance.

 * **npm audit (2026-08-24, three consecutive runs):** totals **44 → 94 → 95**. Critical count stable at **4** (`handlebars`, `shell-quote`, `tar`, `websocket-driver`). Registry results remain non-deterministic for this lockfile; report the observed range, not a single number.

 * **`console.log` in request-path API code** (`items.controller.ts` 50–74) and Hebrew user-facing strings inside API services (`user-profile.service.ts` 294; hierarchy permission errors). Violates project logger + “English only in code” rules.

 * **Operator / Shiduchim Tov still schema-and-guard only.** `OperatorAuthGuard` is implemented; no `/api/operator/*` module, no matching tables, no mobile operator screens (SRS §10.1). Do not treat the guard as a completed feature.

 * **Prior daily-audit PRs #105–#139 are still open and unmerged to `dev`.** Product HEAD has not moved since 2026-08-20. Fixing any single critical (resolve-id, passwordless `/api/users/login`, root-admin bootstrap, register `firebase_uid` bind, mass-assignment/password IDOR including root admin, Google access-token fallback, request-path DROP, committed JWT / DB credential fragments) is higher value than another report-only PR — this run stays report-only because the confirmed blast radius is multi-module and kc-deep-bug-audit forbids low-confidence drive-by fixes.

# 🛡️ Daily Audit Report: 2026-08-23

**Product HEAD audited:** `3d92f9d9cfbf7fa4e02b763eeb62a131d494f040` (merge #101 — identical to `origin/dev` and to the 2026-08-20 / 2026-08-21 / 2026-08-22 audits)
**Audit branch:** `cursor/daily-repository-audit-16d2`
**Scope:** Holistic security / bug / architecture / debt re-verification; secret, DDL, auth-guard, XSS/SQLi, npm, unguarded-controller, login-throttle, login-password-skip, register identity-binding, stats-surface, JWT-refresh-role, chat write-path, Firebase JWKS audience, JwtAuthGuard rate-limit identifier, Google OAuth access-token fallback, profile ownership/password IDOR, and admin-files ACL scans
**SRS mapping:** NFR §3.1–3.2 (`docs/SSOT/SRS/03-non-functional-requirements.md`); auth §2.1, users §2.2, rides §2.4, posts §2.5, tasks, chat §2.7 (`docs/SSOT/SRS/functional/*`); backend §5; gaps §10
**Mode:** Report-only (no product code fixes in this pass — blast radius of confirmed issues is multi-module; prior daily-audit PRs including #138 remain unmerged to `dev`)
**Delta vs 2026-08-22:** No product commits. All previously confirmed critical items re-verified at the same line numbers. New first-class precisions: (1) primary mobile `POST /api/users/login` **skips password verification** when `password` is omitted or `password_hash` is null, then returns `success: true` plus profile PII — `/auth/login` correctly requires and verifies a password; (2) unauthenticated `POST /api/users/register` writes caller-supplied `firebase_uid` **or** `id` onto `user_profiles.firebase_uid`, enabling Firebase-identity pre-binding / hijack before the victim’s first Firebase login. npm audit totals again non-deterministic (**44 → 94 → 95**; critical still **4**: `handlebars`, `shell-quote`, `tar`, `websocket-driver`). Files >200 LOC: **196**.
